Checked: `relics-verify-0..3` (every relic obtained, a card played, a turn ended, each snapshot against the game's own: public 888 snapshots, beta 2,393, none different, ~321,000 reuses on public); with the comparison made blind on purpose (`bdperf relicreuse blind on`) the same check fails at the 3rd snapshot. `snapcheck-5k` 61 and `snapcheck-10k` 31 pairs identical, `sweep relics` A/B identical. The sweep now closes the screen Pandora's Box opens (transformed cards, waiting for its confirm button): before, that relic stopped every relic sweep, with or without this mod.
- `FastListeners` relics of a filtered walk: the trace at 20,580 cards per player had `PileFilter.AddRelics` at 677 ms of 90 s, mostly a concurrent dictionary lookup per relic per walk for filters keeping subscribers (those didn't use the per-player cache). Now one cache per player and filter, valid while the relic `List`'s version stands (every add, insert, removal or set bumps it): whether each relic's type reacts; subscribers still read per relic (a field) for filters keeping them; same relics, same order as `Keeps`. `ab-2k`, `pile-cards-2k`, sweeps of relics and powers identical.
- Combat start, this mod's own share (trace `lad-cstc11`, 10,295 cards per player): `FastListeners.BeforeAdd` was 267 of `PopulateCombatState`'s 689 ms, not the 8-13 ms written above. All of it `LimitlessHookSkip.Note` on each cloned card's MultiEnchantment: Limitless's `EnchantmentsOnCards` getter builds the inner cards from the saved list on first use, and every clone starts without them, so noting built them all (170 ms), then a lock per inner card. Now a (card id, enchantment id) pair seen built gives its type (the game's deserializers and the getter fingerprinted): a saved list of known pairs is noted without building anything; known types are read from a published copy without the lock. `effects-check`: 4,662 notes from saved lists; `ab-2k`, `pile-cards-2k` identical.
- Per-type lookups of filtered walks (trace `lad-trrc22`): `PileFilter.Keeps`/`TypeReacts` asked a concurrent dictionary per relic, potion, power and monster at every walk (~380 ms of 90 s at 20,580 cards per player); on the main thread they now read the filter's per-type-id array (`Reacts`, as pile entries do). `HookFilter.Walked` looks a listener type's override mask up once per run of that type. The live checks of `FastListeners` (a full walk of every pile, ~5 ms at that size, every 2 s per kind: 495 ms of the trace) wait for a moment without an action running, 6 s at most: they land in the frames that took longest. Sweeps of cards, relics, powers, `ab-2k`, `pile-cards-2k`, `shuffle` identical. Rendered co-op without Apotheosis, 2 x 20,580 cards (41,160 in all), before -> after (1% low): rapid plays 90 -> 130 fps, mouse plays 132 -> 155, end of turn 119 -> 133, idle 218, hover 233, median play 92 -> 81 ms; combat start 71 fps with a 1.8 s frame (unchanged). At 10,295 per player within noise (mouse plays 168 then 142 with the same code). So at 40,000 cards in co-op every phase is at 90 fps or more but combat start.
- Combat start, mod on against off in one session (solo, 10,295 cards, headless, two of each): `SetUpCombat` 261-272 ms on, 587-590 ms off; GC pauses during the start 133-201 ms on, 4.5-5.1 s off; whole start 3.4-3.7 s on, 30-32 s off. This mod's per-card weak tables don't make the start's collections worse than the game's own. Trace `lad-cs2c11` (co-op, 2 x 10,295, after the `Note` fix: `FastListeners.BeforeAdd` 267 -> 4 ms): `PopulateCombatState` 629 ms = card cloning 393 (the game's `CardModel.DeepCloneFields`, Limitless's `MultiEnchantment.DeepCloneFields` rebuilding and reserializing every inner card), shuffle 148 (the first filtered walk of the combat, `ModifyShuffleOrder`: segments built, methods compiled), `PileIndex` 88 (a weak table entry per cloned card). What is left is mostly the cloning itself.
- `LimitlessCloneMemo` (part of `limitless_saves`): Limitless's `MultiEnchantment.DeepCloneFields` rebuilds every saved inner card at each clone (`FromSerializable(c).MutableClone().ToSerializable()`, ~210 ms of a 2 x 10,295 co-op combat start). The result depends only on the saved card's content (no code of the game, Limitless or BaseLib writes to a `SerializableCard` after making it; Limitless's `MutableClone` postfix only scales dynamic vars, which aren't saved) and on BaseLib's registered saves (`MemoStamp`). The deck's clones are cloned from the same saved objects each combat: the result made for one is given again. One answer in 256 rebuilt and compared byte for byte. Solo, 10,295 cards, three combat starts in one session: `SetUpCombat` 270 ms then 126 and 153 ms, GC pauses 125 then 34 and 41 ms (the first combat of a session has nothing kept). `combats3-2k-off/on` (three combats, new A/B) 63 lines identical with 3,156 reuses; `autoslay-2k` 613 lines, `party5` 190 lines, `snapcheck-5k` 61 pairs identical.
- `SnapshotCardReuse` pile bits, block copies: a changed pile's bits were composed card by card. Each card's bits depend on its state alone, so the longest runs of the same states at the start and at the end of the previous list are now copied from the previous bits as two blocks (offsets of each card kept with the bits); only the cards between are appended one by one. A first version never copied (it required the offsets array to be exactly as long as the list, but arrays are reused with room to spare): seen by its counter (`Blocks` 0 at 10,000 cards). Fixed: co-op rendered 171,273 cards copied in blocks at 10,295 per player, 353,897 at 20,580. Checksum per play, solo 10,295 cards, 20 each way: mean 2.6 -> 2.2 ms (small: few piles are composed, most reuse their whole bits). `snapcheck-5k` 61 pairs (80,164 cards in blocks), `snapcheck-10k` 31 pairs, `snapcheck-hooks` 11/11, `relics-verify-0` 225/225 identical, no disable. `bdperf blocks on|off`; BENCH `PILE BITS` line (why a pile went through the game's method).
- Remaining leads, all small: `PileIndex`'s weak-table entry per cloned card (88 ms of a 2 x 10,295 co-op combat start); the game's `DynamicVarSet.Clone` (55 ms); the first filtered walk of a combat (`ModifyShuffleOrder`, 142 ms: segments built, JIT); after a whole-deck upgrade (Apotheosis), the next checksum serializes every upgraded card again (~10 ms at 10,295 cards; the upgrade level is 8 bits right after the card's model id, so a card whose only change is its upgrade could take its previous bits with those 8 changed); the action itself (the game upgrading 10,000 cards, 21-27 ms) stays.
- **The game's own reference (`ladder.ps1 -Sizes 0`: the starting deck, nothing imported).** Co-op rendered, 1% low, mod off / on / 10,295 cards per player with the mod (`lad-offc0`, `lad-onc0`, `lad-v9nac11`): idle 721 / 709 / 229 fps, mouse plays 248 / 307 / 168, rapid plays 204 / 210 / 95, end of turn 290 / 338 / 139; combat start worst frame 103 / 121 / 903 ms; one play click to next action 34 / 30 / 81 ms. So the game without this mod already has a ~100 ms frame at every combat start, and a deck-size cost is still there in rapid plays (about half the small deck's rate). The reference run at 945 cards with its relics, mod off: rapid plays 1 fps, mouse plays 10, one play 2.3 s (mod on: 107, 157, 90 ms).
- **Tried and dropped: the `[gold]` text effect in GDScript.** The same co-op trace had the game's C# text effects (`RichTextGold`, `RichTextSine`) at ~25% of the main thread at any deck size: a C# call per glyph per frame, each building a wrapper for that glyph's `CharFXTransform` and registering it in GodotSharp's object tracker (`ConcurrentDictionary.TryAddInternal` 4.8 s of 90 s). Sine was left out from the start: its float math (`MathF.Sin`) can't be proven identical on every machine. Gold (no arithmetic: a color test and a color set) was given to every label as a GDScript effect: 20,000 glyphs identical, and pixels identical (69 texts, 67 of them the reference deck's card descriptions, each drawn by a label with the game's effect and one with the GDScript one, 276 image pairs byte for byte, on both versions; a label without gold differed, so the comparison saw). Rendered co-op, no gain: idle 1% low 237 -> 213 fps at 10,295 cards, mouse plays 149 -> 131, the rest within noise; a GDScript call per glyph costs about as much. Removed.
- `SavedProperties.FromInternal` logs a warning for each null saved property (`TOUCH_OF_OROBAS`' `StarterRelic`, `UpgradedRelic`) at every snapshot: ~224 ms of that trace. The game's log, left as is.

**Menu texts (v0.11.0).** `gc_pacing` now also names the animated texts freeing their memory (English and the 13 languages); `relic_input` (controller actions) and `hook_skip` (relics, potions and powers asked) already said what those options do.

**Equivalence.** Mutations 28/28 as expected; `ab-2k`, `pile-cards-2k`, `shuffle` identical; whole AutoSlay runs `autoslay` 1,517 and `autoslay-2k` 613 lines identical; whole-game sweeps, every potion, relic, power, card and encounter: identical (16,600 checked lines); `release-checks` every `bdperf` comparison identical; `coopfast`, `coopclone`: peers agree; effects 39/40 (`ReplayStateSkip` needs co-op).

**Release checks v0.10.0 (2026-11-03).** `check_versions`: installed and published game (public and beta), BaseLib and Limitless all the verified ones. Both copies, Debug build, no baselines: `release-checks`, `effects-check` (public 45/46, beta 44/45: `ReplayStateSkip` needs co-op), `ab-2k` 54, `pile-cards-2k` 131, `shuffle` 55, `combats3-2k` 63, `hookskip-off/on` 54 lines identical, `autoslay-2k` 613 (public) and 387 (beta) lines identical, `snapcheck-hooks` 11/11; sweeps of every relic, potion, encounter, card and power identical on both (`-Speed fast` relics on public too); `coop`, `coopfast`, `coopclone` peers agree; leaks: `memory-on` flat (public 170-182 MB over 15 turns at 8,400 cards, beta 184-197), `memory-runs-on/off` no static of the mod reaches a left run, positive control reached, menu heap after 4 runs on 176 / off 182 MB (public), 226 / 241 MB (beta); `settings.ps1`, `baselib-optional.ps1`, `limitless-switches.ps1`, `coop-required.ps1` as expected (client refused: "Mods that host has that we don't: BigDeckOptimization-v0.10.0"), user files restored; `solo-8k`, `fast-start-check` (15 waits skipped, 5.2 s) no failure. Public only: `turns-off` vs `hookskip-on` 54 and `pile-cards-off/on` 131 lines identical at 8,400 cards; mutations 28/28 (their expected losses updated for the new counters: `SnapshotCardReuse.Moved` with `Trust`, `Blocks` with `PileBits`, `LimitlessHookSkip.Saved` with pile tracking or `LimitlessHookSkip`, `LimitlessCloneMemo` with `LimitlessReserialize`; every comparison identical in all 28). Release DLL: `tools/compat` 0 problem on both versions. Both copies: `guard-checks` (failing Apply and throwing prefix: OK), `conflict-check` (hook_filter "partly vanilla: also patched by bdtest.fakeconflict"), `guard-async-ref/on` 19 lines identical (FastListeners off after the injected fault), `chemical-x` (Whirlwind 5 hits with the relic, mod on and off, 3 without; the big-deck pair differs only by the Vigor the first attack consumes: 5 x 77 vs 5 x 21), `menu-languages` (13 languages translated, `zht` falls back to English). Release package in game on both copies (zip installed in place of the Debug build, Workshop mods, headless): `Initialized v0.10.0`, 33 patches applied (the Debug build's 35 minus `AutoTest` and `CombatStartTimer`), none skipped, HookSkip 147 (public) / 148 (beta) hooks, 0 left vanilla. Not run: `bdopt` typed by hand in the Release build, SpeedX and ModConfig enabled (not in the account's mod list; every session ran with BaseLib, Limitless, BetterSpire2, CampfireTrade, Unlimited), a real co-op game between two people.
- A first run of that Release check launched the Steam install instead of the copies: the script dot-sourced `parlib.ps1`, whose `common.ps1` sets `$GameDir` to the Steam install, after taking `$GameDir` as its parameter. It unpacked the zip into the Steam install's `mods` (empty before), started that game headless without Steam in a test slot's user data, then removed what it had added: the install is as it was (no local copy, no test mods folder). The script now takes its copy as `-Copy`, refuses a path under `steamapps`, and sets `$GameDir` after dot-sourcing.
- `coop` diverged on the beta in 2 of 4 runs, at checksum 28 each time; the dumps were of two different moments (host still in fight 1, turn 3, an enemy at 0 HP; client at turn 1 of fight 2): the host's debug `kill all` before a second fight made a checksum on the host alone (what `coopfast` already avoids). `coop` is now one combat, `kill all` only as its last action: 4 of 4 runs agree (3 beta, 1 public). Two co-op combat starts stay covered by `coopfast`.
- A save failed to rename once (`autoslay-2k-on`, beta): a mutations series started on the other copy at the same time, and parallel series share the test slots whatever the copy. `parlib.ps1` now takes a lock on the slots; the beta A/B step rerun alone: identical, no error.

### Step S (2026-11-03): what rapid plays still cost; outside-combat scenarios

**Rapid plays, measured on their own.** The ladder's `plays` phases were 4 cards in 0.1-1 s (35-180 frames): their 1% low was the worst frame or two. `ladder.ps1 -PlaysOnly` (per turn: 50 energy, 6 cards drawn, 10 cards played one after the other) and `-What cards|relics` (the first import brings only the reference run's cards or only its relics). Co-op host, rendered, public v0.107.1, all Workshop mods, Apotheosis and Cascade avoided (Cascade with 50 energy auto-plays for over 120 s, the host's script then gives up and the client is left waiting), per turn:

| Host and client each have | avg fps | 1% low | frame p50 | one play |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Starting deck and relics | ~395 | 126-158 | 2.2 ms | 38 ms |
| Starting deck + the reference run's 368 relics | ~215 | 82-121 | 3.7-4.5 ms | 88 ms |
| 945 cards + 368 relics | ~200 | 80-96 | 3.8-4.9 ms | 108 ms |
| 10,295 cards + 368 relics | ~200 | 72-92 | 3.8-4.7 ms | 103 ms |

Deck size no longer shows in rapid plays: 945 and 10,295 cards are the same. What halves the frame rate against the starting deck is the 368 relics each player gets from importing all four players' (the most a real player of that run had is 189): idle frames 1.15 -> 1.96 ms, draw calls 750 -> ~2,200 (the relic bar shows every relic, a node with its icon and label each), plays 1,300 -> ~2,700 draw calls, process step 1.4 -> 2.5 ms. A play takes the same number of frames, each longer. Trace `lad-rptrc11` (co-op, 2 x 10,295, plays only): no dominant C# cost; the co-op checksum is the largest one tied to relics (`RelicStateReuse.Serialize` about half of `FromRun`: each relic's saved properties read again, the game's own call, ~1.7 ms per checksum with 738 relics). The sampler gives the frame's native rendering to the last managed call of the frame (`NScreenShake._Process` "calls" of 80-540 ms, `Stopwatch.GetTimestamp` 55 ms): read those as rendering, not as that method. The relic bar's cost is drawing: nothing to cut without changing what is shown.

**With a real player's relics.** `ladder.ps1 -RelicsFrom 1`: every card of the reference run but only its Ironclad's 189 relics, on each peer (`lad-r189c11`, 11,230 cards per player, the full ladder body, Apotheosis and Cascade avoided), against the 368 relics of `lad-v9nac11` (10,295 cards), 1% low: idle 229 -> 326 fps, hover 240 -> 292, mouse plays 168 -> 198, rapid plays 95 -> 132, end of turn 139 -> 179; one play 81 -> 55 ms median; combat start worst frame 903 -> 812 ms. With relics as a real player has them, every phase of a combat is above 120 fps on the 1% low at 10,000+ cards per player in co-op, combat start aside.

**Outside combat (new scenarios).** Nothing tested the mod in shops, events, rest sites, treasures, rewards or the map with relics, and never in co-op. `autoroom` plays the current room with the game's own auto player (AutoSlay's handlers, found by their interfaces: the same code on both versions), `selector ui` lets the game open its card choice screens (the deck grids) and the handlers answer them, `runstate` logs every player's gold, HP, potions, relics and a hash of the deck. Fights are played by the scenario's own loop (the game's combat handler applies powers outside the action queue: a co-op peer would never see them); the shop by a mirror of v0.111's handler on both versions (v0.107's awaits a purchase without answering the screen it opens). `barrier` (a networked `bigdeck mark`) keeps co-op peers in step. `outside-2k-off/on` (solo, ~1,900 cards, 368 relics, enemies at 1 HP): a fight and its rewards, 4 map rooms, a shop with card removal and every affordable item, smith on the upgrade grid, a treasure, a rest site, 9 events (transform, enchant, upgrade, remove grids), act 2, then the run saved, left for the menu and continued: 48 STATE lines identical mod off and on, the state after loading the same as before leaving. `outside-coop` and `outside-coopoff` (both peers ~1,900 cards and 368 relics, map votes, shop, smith, a treasure vote, 3 events): peers agree on 33 run state lines in both, and the mod-on and mod-off pairs are identical. A first co-op run had the host take the next room before the client read its state (a false difference): a barrier after each read. `par.ps1` now counts a session its first failed line stopped as a problem (two sessions failing at the same line had compared identical). Beta v0.111: the solo shop buys an item that opens the enchant grid, and after its confirm the screen never closes (the game's enchant handler waits; mod off the same, the game idle, no managed code running): the beta's own auto player or game, not this mod; `outside-2k` stops there on the beta. In co-op its upgrade grid of Aroma of Chaos has no card holder the game's handler finds ("No cards found", even after 10 s of tries 0.5 s apart; mod off the same): `outside-coop` stops there on the beta, its 24 run state lines before identical mod off and on, peers agreeing. The drain waits in time anyway (0.1 s between screens, up to 20 tries 0.5 s apart on one that stays), as headless frames take no time.

**`RelicStateReuse.Scalar` (new part of `coop_checksum`).** `bdperf relics` (369 relics, a pass as one co-op checksum makes for one player): every relic type saves `IsWax` and `IsMelted` (bools, saved when true), 47 relic properties are counters; `SavedProperties.From` reads them by reflection with boxing and a new object, ~0.5 ms per pass. For a type whose saved properties are all `bool`, `int` or int-based enums saved always or when not their type's default, compiled getters compare them with the previous object's lists (order, names, values, the same rules as `FromInternal`): equal, the previous object without calling `From`; otherwise the game's call as before. Not for a type a BaseLib "SavedSpireField" targets (its `FromInternal` postfix exports those; Limitless registers Fur Coat and Golden Compass), nor with another mod's patch on `FromInternal` or `ShouldSerialize` (BaseLib's postfix allowed by fingerprint while it targets nothing of that type). Guards read once per `TickCount64` tick, not per relic. One answer in 256 (Debug 16) compared with the game's `From`; a difference turns the part off. Per pass 0.52 -> 0.19 ms (353-360 of 365 relics without `From`). Checked: `relics-verify-0..3` public (~225 snapshots each) and beta (~600 each) identical to the game's own, ~80,000 / ~215,000 relics through the part per session; `sweep relics` A/B 4,455 lines identical; `snapcheck-5k` 61, `snapcheck-10k` 31, `snapcheck-hooks` 11/11 identical; `coopfast`, `coopclone`, `snapcheckcoop`, `outside-coop`: peers agree. Positive control `relics-scalarblind` (every relic taken as unchanged unread): the part's own sample caught it (HAPPY_FLOWER) and `bdperf verify all` saw a mismatch.

**Apotheosis.** `apotheosis-10k` (rendered, solo, 10,295 cards + 368 relics), probes: the action 41.5 ms the first time (JIT of each card type's upgrade code), 21.4 ms after; most of it per card in `CurrentUpgradeLevel`'s setter: Limitless's `CardModelSetPreviousLevel` prefix (a BaseLib field set per dynamic var: Limitless's own logic, left) and, in Debug builds only, this mod's write counter (made cheap: it built a string per write and skewed Debug measures). The co-op checksum after it 31 / 18 ms: every card's snapshot object is new (the light `Upgraded` path) and every card serialized again with its enchantments. **Upgrade bits** (part of `SnapshotCardReuse`'s bits): a card `Upgraded` remade from the very object its kept bits were written for, with the same affliction, cost and keywords, gets its upgrade level written into those bits (`WriteInt(level, 8)` right after the `EntryIdBitSize`-bit entry id: the low byte, least significant bit first) instead of a full serialization. Fingerprints of `CardState.Serialize`, `SerializableCard.Serialize`, `WriteModelEntry`, `WriteInt` (same on v0.111); no transpiler on the card's serialization but Unlimited's (fingerprinted); the first 64 patched cards of a session and then one in 2,048 compared with the game's bits (a difference: `SnapshotCardReuse` off). `snapcheck-apotheosis` (~4,700 cards, two plays checksummed first as a co-op combat does after every action, then Apotheosis four times with plays between): 11 of 11 checksum pairs identical to the game's own; of 22,123 cards the light path remade, 21,996 patched (8,795 of 8,919 at the first Apotheosis): not patched, the upgrade changed the card's keywords (90) or its cost (37), or the card changed twice between checksums (25). Positive control `snapcheck-apotheosis-wrong` (a wrong level written): caught at the first check, pairs still identical. A first reading ("4,700 cards without bits at the first Apotheosis") was the refusal counter counting every card's very first capture at the combat's first snapshot: it now counts upgraded cards only. Cutting an upgraded card's old bits out of its pile's bits was tried for that and removed (no card needed it). An object remade in full clears `UpgradedFrom`. Rendered A/B at 10,295 cards with plays first, two sessions each: checksum after the first Apotheosis 20 -> 8 ms, after the second 18 -> 8 ms (without plays first, the first: 31 -> 21 ms, the snapshot then the combat's second).

**Equivalence after the scalar relics, upgrade bits and `RichTextEnvRelease` (2026-11-03).** Public: `snapcheck-5k` 61, `snapcheck-10k` 31, `snapcheck-apotheosis` 11 pairs identical, `snapcheck-hooks` 11/11, `relics-verify-0..3` 891 snapshots identical, `ab-2k` 54 and `outside-2k` 48 lines identical, `sweep relics` 4,455 lines identical, effects-check only `ReplayStateSkip` at 0 (co-op only), `snapcheck-apotheosis-wrong` caught; `coopfast`, `coopclone`, `snapcheckcoop` peers agree. Beta: `relics-verify-0..3`, `snapcheck-apotheosis`, `snapcheck-hooks`, `ab-2k` 54 lines identical. Beta `coopfast`, `coopclone`: peers agree; `party5-off/on` 190 lines identical (mod off 869 s at that size: `-StallSec 1200`). `outside-coop` on public stopped in the shop this time: a purchase opened the enchant grid allowing no card at all ("up to N", `MinSelect` 0); the game's auto-player handler then leaves its selection loop with none chosen (the main confirm is already enabled), confirms an empty preview, and the screen's `ConfirmSelection` does nothing without a selection: it never closes (mod off the same; also the beta's solo hang). `autoroom` now answers that screen itself: one random card, then the confirm of whichever preview shows. Then: `outside-coop` and `outside-coopoff` 33 run state lines, peers agree, on public and on the beta. Beta solo `outside-2k` then stopped, mod on and off at the same line, at Aroma of Chaos's upgrade grid (the deck cloned by a rest site option, 1,987 -> 6,547 cards, was not the cause). A `SCREEN` line (`autoroom` describes a screen its handler can't close: cards offered, min/max, nodes) showed it hidden: on v0.111 a screen pushed while the map is open is hidden at once (`NOverlayStack.StackIsCovered`), the handlers skip hidden screens, and the scenario's debug jumps (`cmd event`, `cmd room`) leave the map open after a rest site's proceed, as travel never does. `autoroom` closes a map left open on another room. Next stop: `runstate` waiting for a combat's start never saw it idle: the first turn asked a card choice (a relic's "up to N", min 0), and with `selector ui` only `autoroom`'s fights had the automatic selector. With `selector ui`, every fight now gets it from `CombatSetUp` to `CombatEnded`. Beta solo `outside-2k-off/on`: 48 lines identical, the state after loading the same as before leaving (6,566 cards). Co-op: two peers playing at once interleave their actions by timing, and the rewards' random draws with it: peers agree within a session, but mod-off and mod-on sessions differed from the first fight's rewards. In `autoroom` fights only the host plays now (the client ends its turns): public `outside-coop` vs `outside-coopoff` 33 lines identical. The shop's state read differed between peers the same way in both sessions (another player's last purchase reaches this peer when it leaves the shop): that read is the local player's only (`runstate shop local`, not compared between peers), and a 3 s wait follows each barrier before a read. An event can go on after the handler's single choice (Dense Vegetation: rest, then a page with only "Fight", a fight in the event room): `autoroom` plays further pages and event fights until a proceed option or the map. In co-op `cmd room` is queued, and `waitfor room` passed on the previous room's node (the script then touched it freed, "Cannot access a disposed object"): it now waits for the room's own node by name (`MerchantRoom`, `RestSiteRoom`, `TreasureRoom`, `EventRoom`). Final: `outside-2k-off/on` 48 lines identical on public and beta; `outside-coop` and `outside-coopoff` peers agree on 30 compared lines on both, and the mod-off and mod-on sessions are identical (32 lines each peer) on both.

**Ephemeral collections of 30-95 ms, mod off too (`RichTextEnvRelease`, new part of `gc_pacing`).** Every co-op ladder session showed, on the map before the fight, a gen1 collection every 2-5 s of 70-95 ms (starting deck, mod off: 82-87 ms; 11,230 cards: 77-97 ms), and 30-70 ms ones in small-deck combats (where `GcPacing`, from 1,000 cards, doesn't run). `mapidle` (starting deck, map, 30 s, uncapped ~2,600 fps), trace `gc-verbose`, `allocs --gc`: each gen1's time was the "new finalization queue" mark step (dead objects with finalizers kept alive with what they reach: 13 MB promoted each time); allocations: 174 of 227 MB from `RichTextSine._ProcessCustomFX`: its `charFx.Env` gives a new `Godot.Collections.Dictionary` (a finalizer, a `WeakReference` and a node in GodotSharp's disposables tracker) per glyph per frame. Five of the game's text effects read `Env` that way (sine, fade-in, fly-in, jitter, thinking dots); none keeps it beyond the call (fingerprints, the same on v0.111). The patch disposes of it when the effect returns: what its finalizer would do later, done on the main thread after its last use; same values read, same pixels. Map, two sessions each, off -> on: collections 5 gen1 -> 4 gen0 + 1 gen1, each 65-87 -> 20-31 ms, 338-353 -> 115-120 ms of pauses in 30 s, worst frame 83-86 -> 28-29 ms, frames over 33 ms 5 -> 0. ~70,000 dictionaries a second at these frame rates (a session of 140 s: ~10 million). What is left of each pause is the tracker's `WeakReference` per dictionary (also finalizable): avoiding it means not making the dictionary at all (GodotSharp's internal interop), left. Co-op ladder at 945 cards, one session each: no collection fell inside the measured phases; 1% low off -> on: hover 290 -> 295 (worst 47 -> 11 ms), mouse plays 147 -> 166, plays 101 -> 115, end of turn 168 -> 188 (within the noise of single sessions). `GcPacing` from 0 cards (`bdperf gcmin 0`): no gain at 945 cards (no collection in the phases), left at 1,000. Collection frequency follows the frame rate (allocation per frame): uncapped test sessions collect several times more often than a game at 144 fps; their length doesn't.

**Apotheosis, again (2026-11-03 evening).** Co-op rendered, 11,230 cards per player, 189 relics, Apotheosis allowed (`lad-apoc11`): an Apotheosis frame is ~26 ms (the action ~17 ms, the co-op checksum after it ~8 ms, one frame); rapid plays with 4-5 Apotheoses per turn: 1% low 33-41 fps, 91 with one. A cpu-sampling trace put 69 of 74 ms of three Apotheoses in Limitless's `PreviousLevel` (a `ConditionalWeakTable.AddOrUpdate` per dynamic variable). **Wrong: EventPipe's sample profiler stops threads at GC safe points**, so samples gather at allocations, FCALLs and calls, not where time goes; the same bias later put 180 ms on `List<RelicState>` growth and 350 ms on Debug counters of the checksum. Read its trees for what calls what, and time with stopwatches (`PerfProbe`, `bdperf` benches) before believing a leaf. `bdperf upgradebench` (in combat, the local player's cards, nothing changed; scenario `upgradebench-10k`): Limitless's prefix 1.3-1.5 ms per pass of 28,000 variables (its first pass, adding the entries, 1.5), the table read 0.4 ms; on fresh copies of the same 10,260 cards: `IsUpgradable` 0.8 ms, `UpgradeInternal` 9-11 (level set with every prefix 2.2, `OnUpgrade` 1.4, BaseLib's variable upgrades 1.5, calculated variables 0.75, the rest BaseLib's modifiers postfix), `FinalizeUpgradeInternal` 0.8-1.7. ~1.6 µs per card spread over the game, BaseLib and Limitless, no step above 2 ms. **Tried and dropped: `LimitlessPreviousLevel`** (Limitless's two prefixes mirrored, the table read without its lock, an unchanged level not written, a level written into its box): A/B of the action 28/18/17 ms on and off alike; the whole prefix is ~1.4 ms. Under 8 ms for that frame would take spreading the upgrade over frames, which changes when the game's state changes: not done. **Debug builds counted every card write by method** through Harmony's `__originalMethod` (a method lookup per call): ~4 ms per Apotheosis at 10,300 cards, in every Debug measure of an action writing cards. Now only with `--bdwrites` or `BD_WRITES=1` (`WRITES` lines, with the pile of each written card); an Apotheosis action 20.5 -> 17 ms in Debug.

**What the checksum still read in rapid plays: written piles in full.** Probes in `PilePrefix` (`PILE TIMES` per pile and path, `bdperf piletimes [reset]`, `ladder.ps1 -Probe` logs them per turn with `bdperf costhooks`): at 11,230 cards per player the draw pile was read in full 35-58 times per session, 6.4-9 ms each, mostly "written" (`SetThisTurn` and upgrades on draw pile cards from the cards played: one written card, 10,000 read) and, once Curious was played, at every checksum (a cost listener: every pile read in full, 288 reads of ~4.6 ms in `lad-dron1c11`, rapid plays 1% low 41-63 from then on). **Dirty reads** (`SnapshotCardReuse`, bits part, `bdperf dirtyreads on|off`): a write marks the card's entry in the pile it was last read in (up to 256 per pile, more: the next read reads every card); a pile with written cards takes the moved-pile path, reading again only the marked cards and keeping the others' states. **Pure listeners** (`bdperf purehooks on|off`): with cost or keyword listeners active, a pile stays trusted while the same listeners stand with the same amounts and owners, when every one of them answers only from what a read covers (card owner, type, base cost and local modifiers, keywords, affliction, pile type; the listener's amount and owner): Curious, Borrowed Time, Corruption, Spiked Gauntlets, Free Attack/Skill/Power, Tangled, Hex, Veilpiercer (fingerprints, the same on v0.111; not Void Form or Brilliant Scarf, which count this turn's plays). Draw pile in rapid plays then: dirty or moved reads at ~0.55 ms, full reads only at turn changes (end-of-turn cleanup writes every card).

**A hidden write, found by the dirty reads (a bug of every released version with BaseLib).** Co-op with dirty reads on one peer and off on the other diverged after Bullet Time (`droff1/2`: the client ran its `dirtyreads off` line after those checksums); on/on didn't (both peers wrong the same way). `snapcheck-freeturn` (solo, verify all) reproduced it: after Bullet Time the hand's cards lacked their `Energy Cost: 0`. `bdperf costwrites`: `SetThisTurnOrUntilPlayed` called directly reached the write prefix, through `SetToFreeThisTurn` it didn't. BaseLib postfixes `SetToFreeThisTurn`; Harmony compiled that replacement when BaseLib loaded, before this mod protected `SetThisTurnOrUntilPlayed` from inlining, and its body was inlined there. The old full read of a written pile hid it only when another counted write fell in the same pile; a trusted or moved pile with only such writes kept stale costs. **`InlineGuard.RecompileInliners`**: once every patch is in, each Harmony-patched method whose code calls a protected method (directly or through small non-virtual callees, two levels) is rebuilt by Harmony with the same patches (`new PatchProcessor(h, m).Patch()` adds nothing: null patches are dropped), now compiled with the flags set: 96 methods, ~0.3 s at load; one (`NRewardButton._Ready`, a BaseLib postfix) fails to rebuild ("invalid program") and stays as compiled. `snapcheck-freeturn` then 6/6 identical with dirty reads on and off; co-op verify ladder at 2 x 1,870 cards (`lad-vfon2c2`): no mismatch over two turns (it then times out: a vanilla re-check per action takes 2 s there).

**Measures and equivalence after dirty reads, pure listeners and the recompile (2026-11-03 night).** Co-op rendered, 11,230 cards per player, rapid plays, `dirtyreads off` on the host (the case that diverged) and `on`, two sessions each (`droff3/4`, `dron3/4`): no divergence; draw pile read in full 55-57 -> 21 times a session (the rest at turn changes); rapid plays 1% low 70-140 by turn either way (within noise). The checksum is no longer what the worst frames of rapid plays are made of: `bdperf probe nodes` (every node callback probed, and the engine entries `PackedScene.Instantiate`, `AddChild`, `RemoveChild`, `Reparent`, `CreateTween`) covers ~30% of the 10-12 ms frames (`lad-nodc11`: 55 hitch frames, 642 ms; the action 41, checksum 17, `NRun._Process` 12, hand update 11, `Reparent` 11, `Instantiate` 5 ms); the rest is the engine's own work and async continuations. The game's C# text effects, probed at their bridge entry (the glyph's wrapper built there): Gold 9.3 million calls, 1.4 s over a whole ladder session, all of them ~0.1 ms a frame, not the ~25% the sampler showed (Step R). Finding where the rest of those frames goes needs a native profiler (ETW kernel sampling: admin rights). Public: `snapcheck-purehooks` 15/15 verified (33 piles kept with Curious and Corruption active), `snapcheck-freeturn` and `-old` 6/6, `snapcheck-hooks` 11/11, `snapcheck-apotheosis` 11, `snapcheck-5k` 61, `snapcheck-10k` 31 pairs identical, `ab-2k` 54 and `outside-2k` 48 lines identical; `snapcheck-purehooks-wrong` (listeners compared without amounts) caught; `coopfast`, `coopclone`, `snapcheckcoop`, `outside-coop`, `outside-coopoff`: peers agree, `outside-coop` on and off 32 lines identical per peer. Beta: `snapcheck-purehooks` 24/24, `freeturn` and `-old` 8/8, `hooks` 31/31, `apotheosis` 11, `5k` 61 pairs, `ab-2k` 54 and `outside-2k` 48 lines identical, the positive control caught, the co-op five agree with `outside-coop` on/off identical; `RecompileInliners` 98 methods there, the same `NRewardButton._Ready` left. `par.ps1` didn't see a failed `cmd bdperf verify check` (a console command's failure is only printed): it does now.

**Release checks v0.11.0 (2026-11-03).** `check_versions`: installed and published game (public and beta), BaseLib and Limitless all the verified ones. Both copies, Debug build, no baselines: `release-checks` (checksum, refresh, grid, tabs, pool, piles: OK identical), `effects-check` 49/51 (`SnapshotCardReuse.Hooks` needs an active cost listener: `snapcheck-purehooks` 33 on public, 63 on beta; `ReplayStateSkip` co-op only on public, `ControllerActionSkip` on beta, whose game no longer logs the missing controller actions), `ab-2k` 54, `pile-cards-2k` 131, `shuffle` 55, `combats3-2k` 63, `hookskip-off/on` 54, `chemical-x-big` 23, `autoslay-2k` 613 (public) and 387 (beta) lines identical, `guard-async-ref/on` 19 identical, `snapcheck-hooks`, `-purehooks`, `-freeturn` all verified identical; sweeps without baselines: relics 4,455 / 4,485, potions 328 / 333, encounters 3,146 / 3,321, cards 3,452 / 3,555, powers 5,240 / 5,342 lines identical (public / beta; items stopping a session do so mod off too), `-Speed fast` relics on public identical; `coop`, `coopfast`, `coopclone` peers agree on both, `coop.ps1 coopfast -WithMods` no divergence and the client's waits skipped; leaks: `memory-on` flat (public 168-181 MB over 15 turns at 8,400 cards, beta 187-197), `memory-runs-on/off` no static of the mod reaches a left run, the positive control reached, menu heap after 4 runs on 178 / off 183 MB (public), 228 / 243 MB (beta); `settings.ps1`, `baselib-optional.ps1`, `limitless-switches.ps1` all ok, `coop-required.ps1` refused ("Mods that host has that we don't: BigDeckOptimization-v0.11.0"), user files restored; `solo-8k`, `fast-start-check` (15 waits skipped, 5.2 s) no failure; `guard-checks` OK, `conflict-check` (hook_filter "partly vanilla"), `chemical-x` as before; `menu-languages` rendered (`run.ps1`: headless can't capture): 13 languages translated, `zht` English. Public only: `turns-off` vs `hookskip-on` 54 and `pile-cards-off/on` 131 lines identical at 8,400 cards; mutations 28/28 once their expected losses took the new counters (`SnapshotCardReuse.Dirty` with `Moved`, `UpgradeBits` with the other bits), every comparison identical. Release DLL: `tools/compat` 0 problem on both versions. Release package in game on both copies (`release-incopy.ps1`: the zip in place of the Debug build, Workshop mods, headless, the Debug build put back): `Initialized v0.11.0`, 35 patches applied, none skipped, failed or disabled, `InlineGuard` 96 (public) / 98 (beta) methods compiled again (one left), HookSkip 147 / 148 hooks, 0 left vanilla. Not run: `bdopt` typed by hand in the Release build, SpeedX and ModConfig (not in the account's mod list), a real co-op game between two people.

### Step T (2026-11-03): the first native profile

WPR (`CPU` profile, 1 kHz, CPU samples and context switches with stacks) around a rendered co-op session (`lad-etwc11`, public v0.107.1, 2 x 11,230 cards, 189 relics, 60 rapid plays, Apotheosis and Cascade avoided). Tools: `tools/profiling/etwdump`, `dwarfsyms`, `etwreport.py`. `SlayTheSpire2.exe` (MegaDot 4.5.1) has no .pdb but carries its DWARF: 121,425 functions named. C# frames are named too; `coreclr` and `libspine_godot` are not. Sampling costs little: combat start worst frame 842 ms with it, 761-842 ms without.

**Rapid plays (4.4 s).** The main thread is on the CPU 97% of the time: the GPU isn't what limits them (waits 120 ms, 116 of them below). `SceneTree::process` 56%, `RenderingServerDefault::_draw` 40.5% (`swap_buffers` 23.6%, mostly `RenderingDeviceGraph::end`; `draw_viewports` 13.9%). `CanvasItem::_redraw_callback` 24.7%: `RichTextLabel` 13%, Spine 6.8%, `Line2D` 3%. Spine in all 12.1% (idle 6.2%, self only).

58 frames of 8 ms or more (longest 28 ms; 637 of 4,342 samples): `SceneTree::process` 72%, redraws 32.7%, Spine 24.2%, C# continuations 24.2%, `RichTextLabel` 8.8%, kernel 13.5%.

- **Spine reads meshes back from the GPU.** `libspine_godot` calls `RenderingServer.mesh_get_surface`, which ends in `RenderingDevice::buffer_get_data`: 15.5% of those frames' samples, and every GPU wait of the phase (2,192 waits of up to 1 ms, 116 ms). With its CPU time 244 ms of 4.4 s (5.5%); at idle 5 samples of 7,962. Native code of the game's Spine library, out of Harmony's reach; not measured at other deck sizes. No way to avoid it found yet.
- **Squeeze counts the whole deck at each calculation.** Its `CalculatedDamageVar` multiplier: `AllCards.Count(c => c.Tags.Contains(OstyAttack) ...)`, for each Squeeze shown: 2.8% of rapid plays, 6.4% of those frames. The one deck-size cost seen in them, and the reference run's deck is mostly Squeeze. Fixed: `DeckCountMemo` below.

**Combat start: three long frames, 419, 840 and 196 ms (sampled).** 60% of their samples are self time in `coreclr`, invisible to the EventPipe sampler. 419 ms: `OnSyncPlayerMessageReceived` (the peer's deck deserialized, co-op only). 840 ms: `SetUpCombat` 593 samples, `PopulateCombatState` 549, `DeepCloneFields` 463 (`DynamicVarSet.Clone` 317). 196 ms: the first checksum (`FromRun`). In 50 ms slices `coreclr` comes in solid blocks of 90-100% (about 350 ms of the 840 ms frame): collections, reached from the allocation sites of the cloning. Without `coreclr` symbols the rest of its time isn't split (allocation paths, casts, dependent handles).

The clones survive, so collections copy them instead of freeing anything. `GcPacing` doesn't cover this moment (`IsQuiet` excludes `IsStarting`). `DOTNET_GCgen0size=40000000` (1 GB, test copy, two sessions each way): collections 10-11 -> 6 gen0, but worst frame 842 / 761 -> 731 / 740 ms, pauses in the window 715-730 -> 648-678 ms: fewer collections copy nearly as much, and one gen2 remains. Not an upper bound for a no-GC region, tried below. `gc-verbose` confirms the shape (gen1 collections promoting 25-28 MB each) but inflates pauses threefold (2,686 ms against ~715): use it for structure, not times.

**No-GC region over the cloning (experiment, removed).** `TryStartNoGCRegion` before `SetUpCombat`, 2 x 11,230 cards, host, two sessions each way against the same code without it: no collection during it (189 MB allocated), `SetUpCombat` 590-670 -> 333 ms, worst frame 716-818 -> 478-499 ms, the region itself 76-84 ms whatever its size (400 MB or 1.5 GB). The cost moves, as feared: the clones still have to be promoted. Held until the first quiet moment and collected there (`hreg`): one frame of ~280 ms as the first turn opens; pauses in the window 623-689 -> 483-500 ms. Left to `GcPacing` (`BD_GCSTART_NOCOLLECT`, `ireg`): pauses 646-655 -> 198-246 ms during the start, but the collection then falls anywhere: a 363 ms frame in the middle of rapid plays in one session of two. Not an option: an action's freeze is worse than a loading one. The choice left was one frame of ~750 ms or two of ~490 and ~280 ms: decided with the user (2026-11-04), one freeze stays, two separate ones would feel odd to a player for so little. Code removed. Tested on public only, peers agreed in those sessions.

**Which cards still cost more with the deck (2026-11-03/04).** `scalereport.py` of 11-02 ranked Catastrophe, Hidden Gem, Anointed, Uproar, Reboot at +100 to +700 ms a play: out of date. `deckcards-1k` and `deckcards-10k` (rendered, instant speed, each card three times), ms at ~950 / ~10,300 cards: Catastrophe 242-174 / 291-171, Hidden Gem 62-104 / 67-116, Uproar 112-150 / 121-155, Anointed 88-185 / 117-182, Reboot 155-412 / 177-419, All for One 75-111 / 86-108, Seeker Strike 71-108 / 73-92, Havoc 117-150 / 100-171; only Cascade still shows the deck, 307-326 / 392-420. `StableShuffle` sorts the whole pile but that isn't the cost: `bdperf shufflebench` (scenario `shufflebench-10k`), 2.85 ms at 10,309 cards, 1.42 ms on integer keys (same order, 40 starts): not worth a patch, and patching that generic method would reach every reference type sharing its code. Decks of one card (`bigdeck 5000 <id>`, its limit) can't show these costs through `played X in`: a play reads ~103 ms at 100 and 5,000 copies alike, a fixed wait; only a chain does: Uproar auto-plays its copies, 3,131 in a row, 56 ms a play at 100 copies, 67 ms then bursts of 60-425 ms at 5,000 (four sessions at once, no steady growth, nested awaits 3,000 deep: not read as a deck cost). Cards reading the whole deck at each calculation: Squeeze, Perfected Strike, Crescent Spear (0.3-0.4 ms a count at 10,300 cards). Claw and Maul walk it once per play.

**`DeckCountMemo` (new part of `combat_refresh`).** The count of those three cards is kept per player and card, as of a `PileVersion`; when every pile change since is in `FastListeners`' moves those are applied to it (`ApplyMoves`), else the piles are counted as the game does. Tags and star cost are fixed in a card (`_tags` written once, only mocks override them; no mod touches these cards or their tags). Squeeze leaves itself out through `PileIndex.PlacesIn`; unsure, the game's code runs. Prefix on the compiler's lambdas, found by what they take and return, fingerprinted with what they count (same on v0.111). One answer in 256 (Debug 16) lets the game's code run and compares; a difference turns it off. `deckcount-on` (the 59 pile-scanning cards with the three in hand, then a turn change, every answer checked, `bdperf deckcount verify 1`): public 3,129 and beta 3,374 answers identical to the game's, 6 walks; `deckcount-off/on` 131 and `ab-2k` 54 lines identical on both, `pile-cards-2k`, `shuffle` on public; `deckcount-wrong` (answers off by one) caught on both; `coopfast`, `coopclone` peers agree on both; mutations 28/28 (`DeckCountMemo` and `.Checks` leave with pile tracking); `effects-check` 578 answers, 37 checked. Co-op rendered, 2 x 11,230 cards, same 60 plays traced before and after (`lad-etwc11`, `lad-etw2c11`): Squeeze 120 -> 15 samples of the main thread, 41 -> 1 in frames of 8 ms or more. Rapid plays 1% low 62-88 -> 45-72 fps, one session each: phases of ~150 frames, read as noise, no gain shown in fps (about 2.5% of the phase's time was removed).

### Step U (2026-11-04): several combats in co-op, and what a clone rebuilds

Every co-op session until now played one combat (the ladder) or a few short ones at small sizes: nothing could see what a run's next combats inherit from the last. Probes on the sync (`ladder.ps1 -ProbeStart`) and `coopleak` / `coopfights10k` (several combats in a row, `gcfull` between them; `gcfull` also logs `RunState._allCards`) did.

**A game leak in co-op (`SyncedCardRelease`, new part of `gc_pacing`).** At every co-op room entry (any map point not already finished: fights, events, shops, rest sites, treasure; and a room entered without leaving the current one, an event's fight), through `CombatStateSynchronizer.WaitForSync` before the room is made, the game rebuilds every other player's deck from their message (`Player.SyncWithSerializedPlayer`: `Deck.Clear`, then `RunState.LoadCard` per card; first written here as "at each combat start", corrected by the 2026-11-15 fourth audit pass). The replaced cards never leave `RunState._allCards`: the run keeps every other player's whole deck once per room, mod on or off. `coopleak`, 1,900 cards per player: run cards 3,762 -> 15,282 over 6 fights, +23 MB per fight mod off (`coopleak` alternates fights, so per sync). The previous room is exited before the sync and an event resumed after its fight reads the current deck: a replaced card stays unreachable across rooms. Now the cards that left the deck during that call are removed from the list in one pass. The list is only read by `ContainsCard` (cards being added to a deck, or just made): a replaced card is unreachable. Fingerprint of the sync (same on v0.111), and every user of `_allCards` checked at load (a new reader leaves this vanilla).

**This mod kept finished combats alive (`SnapshotCardReuse`, since v0.8.0).** With that list fixed the heap didn't move. `dotnet-dump gcroot` of old `Squeeze` objects: the game's last 20 checksum states -> their pile lists -> `_byList` -> `PileLast.Lasts` -> `Last.Card`, and `_bySaved` -> `Last` the same way: a finished combat's cards lived on through the next one (in a long combat only the last one; here all of them, each fight making few checksums). `Player.AfterCombatEnd` now releases each pile's entries (`PileLast.Release`, counter `SnapshotCardReuse.Release`).

Heap after that (`coopleak`, 26 fights): +6 MB per fight up to fight 20, then flat (272.8 -> 275.7 MB over 6 fights, the reward cards). The slope was the game's own 20 states filling (9 `NetFullCombatState` alive after 8 fights, a fight here makes one or two checksums).

**`LimitlessCloneMemo` by content.** `allocs --under <method>` (new: only what a method's stack allocated): `PopulateCombatState` 83 MB at 10,295 cards, 45 of them Limitless's `DeepCloneFields` (rebuilding each inner card), which the memo only answered from the second combat on and never for other players (keyed by object; their decks are new objects each combat). A big deck holds few different inner cards: the memo is now a dictionary by content (`SameContent`: ids, level, floor, amounts, saved properties, cards in them by reference, and what BaseLib holds for the card: none, or known empty, anything else not kept), no weak table, at most 16,384 contents. Clones now share the result object, read-only like every saved card. `clonememo-check` (`bdperf clonememo verify 1`, three combats): 4,741 answers rebuilt and compared byte for byte, identical.

**`LimitlessHookSkip.AnyInner` built what it could read.** 17 of those 83 MB: the first filtered walk of a combat asked each clone's inner enchantment types through Limitless's getter, which builds the inner cards from the saved list. Nothing else builds them at that moment. The (card, enchantment) pairs seen now keep their type: a list of known pairs is answered from the saved list.

`PopulateCombatState` 2 x 10,296 cards: 687 -> 357 ms, its allocations a quarter of what they were.

| Co-op, 2 x 10,295 cards, 189 relics, host, rendered | before | after |
|---|---|---|
| Combat start, worst frame (`lad-ps1c11`, `lad-ps2c11`, `lad-finc11`) | 812-890 ms | 524-542 ms |
| GC pauses in it | 606 ms | 468 ms |
| 8 combats in a row: worst frame, first / last (`coopfights10kold`, `coopfights10k`) | 972 / 2,030 ms | 626 / 591 ms |
| 8 combats: live heap at the end | 1,279 MB | 526 MB (its slope stops once the game's 20 states are full) |

Solo, a whole AutoSlay run at 10,300 cards (`autoslay-10k-on`): no room entry above 309 ms (Step R: 450-635 ms for combats).

**Tried and dropped: `DynamicVarSet.Clone` without LINQ.** 109,000 calls at that combat start (card, enchantment, each inner card), ~2 us each. A mirror (the dictionary copied, values replaced in place, no iterators, each name hashed once): alternating in one session, 10,309 cards a pass, game 13.5 and 20.8 ms, mirror 29.7 and 21.4 ms, 660-1,015 B against 711 B a card. What costs is `DynamicVar.Clone` itself. A session-to-session comparison would have shown nothing either way: combat start worst frame moves +-50 ms between identical sessions.

**Four players (`coop4.ps1`, new; every other co-op tool stops at two).** A host and three clients on one lobby, `--clientId 1001..1003`, each client's script from one template. `coop4` (1,900 cards each, four rounds, plays on every peer) and `coop4leak` (four fights, three decks rebuilt at each): every peer the same states, no divergence, run cards flat (7,526 -> 7,718, the reward cards), `SyncedCardRelease` 22,723. `coop4-10k` (`-Rendered`: host rendered, three clients headless on the same PC; 4 x 10,300 cards, 41,186 in the combat, 189 relics each): peers agree; combat start worst frame 1,169 ms; idle 319 fps (1% low 202); end of turn 1% low 83-105 fps, worst 26-43 ms; rapid plays 142-207 fps, 1% low 22-59 (70-170 frames a window, worst 18-45 ms). Probed, no dominant cost in those frames: checksum 2.7 ms on average for a state of 41,000 cards, the rest engine.

**A long combat (`ladder.ps1 -PlaysOnly -Turns 25`, co-op, 2 x 11,230 cards).** 18 rounds until both players died (the cultists' strength grows; the session then waits at the game over screen, not a hang): rapid plays 1% low 92-109 fps from first to last, worst frame 11-23 ms, no degradation. The `heap` of `gcstat` climbs to 1.2 GB there (`GcPacing` holds collections back during plays), after a collection it reads ~570 MB, against 433 MB at the start.

**Idle frames: the tracker of `charFx.Env` (`RichTextEnvRelease.Untracked`).** Of 678 MB allocated in 34 s on the map, 659 MB came from `RichTextEnvRelease.Env`: the Dictionary wrapper 214 MB and, for its entry in GodotSharp's `DisposablesTracker`, a `WeakReference` 167 MB and a concurrent dictionary node 278 MB. Disposing of the dictionary (Step S) doesn't suppress the `WeakReference`'s own finalizer: the "new finalization queue" step stayed the bulk of each ephemeral collection, ~30 ms each, every few seconds at uncapped rates. These dictionaries live for one call, and the tracker only serves engine shutdown: a prefix on `DisposablesTracker.RegisterDisposable` returns null while one is read (`Dictionary.Dispose` checks for null; fingerprints of both, same on v0.111; the finalizer stays as a net). `mapidle`, 30 s: collections 4 gen0 + 1 gen1 of 20-31 ms -> 3 gen0 + 2 gen1 of 7-17 ms, pauses 115-120 -> 37 ms, worst frame 28-29 -> 17 ms.

Equivalence: `ab-2k` 54, `combats3-2k` 63, `party5` 190 lines identical, `snapcheck-5k`, `snapcheck-hooks` identical, `coopfast`, `coopclone`, `coopleak` peers agree.

**A client's frames (`coop4.ps1 -Peers 2 -RenderedClient`, `coopclient10k`).** All co-op numbers so far were the host's. The client, rendered, 2 x 11,230 cards, 1% low: idle 303 fps, mouse plays 201, rapid plays 112, end of turn 161; combat start worst frame 445 ms; one play 60 ms median. The same behaviour as the host's.

**Final code, public, co-op rendered, 189 relics per player, Apotheosis and Cascade avoided (`lad-finc11`, `lad-finc22`), 1% low:**

| Cards per player | idle | hover | mouse plays | rapid plays | end of turn | combat start | its worst frame | one play, median |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 11,230 | 349 | 295 | 220 | 104 | 190 | 119 | 524 ms | 55 ms |
| 21,515 | 303 | 269 | 191 | 88 | 159 | 92 | 885 ms | 62 ms |

The second row is 43,030 cards in the combat: 1% low 88 fps or more in every phase, the worst frame 885 ms, no divergence (Step Q at 20,580 per player: 1,716 ms). That is about the condition set with the user for the 32-bit card id (Step Q, 1% low >= 90 in every phase at 40,000 cards): rapid plays are a window of ~370 frames, 88 is within noise of 90.

Leads left: none worth a patch. The combat start is the game's cloning and rebuilding, ~8 us a card outside collections; `PileIndex`'s weak table entry per clone (a dictionary instead would need its own lifetime rules, ~60 ms of 524); avoiding the rebuild of another player's deck when unchanged (~170 ms a peer at 10,300 cards) can't be shown equal to a fresh load: deck cards hold state no save carries. In frames of actions, engine work, Step T.

**Audit (2026-11-04).** `tools/fpdiff deps` and a walk through each patch: what it uses, what it pins, how it falls back.
- Pinned now: `IdleButtonInput` kept idle buttons from receiving input without pinning `NButton._Input` or `CheckMouseDragThreshold` (a game update adding anything there would have stopped working silently); `FastCombatStart` skips `Cmd.Wait` and `CustomScaledWait` without pinning that their code only waits. The same on both versions. Mutations `button-input`, `wait-body`, plus `sync-body`, `allcards-reader`, `squeeze-count`, `limitless-clone` for this step's patches.
- Reviewed, nothing to pin: patches that only wrap a call with the same result whatever the code around it (`ControllerActionSkip`, `ThemeColorSkip`, `RoomCollect`, `SavedPropertyLookup`), UI-only cleanups (`PooledCardRelease`, `ModImage`), `GcPacing` (no game logic). The unpinned methods left in `deps` are plain getters, invalidation entry points or scopes, as in the v0.7.0 audit.
- `Settings`: an option's "not applied (game code changed)" read a patch's `Applied` flag, down while patches still apply over the first frames: the settings line of every log said so for options that were fine a second later. Those now say it only once every patch was tried.
- Startup, Release build: `Initialized` 5 ms, then 377 ms of patches, as before. `InlineGuard.RecompileInliners` (v0.11) ran its ~300 ms in one frame, unbudgeted: now a step per patched method on the same budget as the patches, at once if a run is under way (38 frames rendered). `NRewardButton._Ready` can't be rebuilt (Harmony: invalid program, another mod's transpiler): left as compiled, as logged, nothing of this mod's writes runs there. (Wrong, found 2026-11-10: the failed rebuild had removed BaseLib's postfix there, see "Harmony rebuilds that dropped another mod's patches".)
- Package: 505 KB zipped, DLL 548 KB (fingerprints and HookSkip's table, translations 103 KB, embedded PDB 42 KB), image 242 KB. Nothing worth what it would cost (Step P's reasons stand).
- Test tooling: `allocs --under`, `ladder.ps1 -ProbeStart`, `coop4.ps1`, `coopleak`, `coopfights10k`, `clonememo-check`, `gcfull` with the run's card list.

**Release checks v0.12.0 (2026-11-04).** `check_versions`: installed and published game (public and beta), BaseLib and Limitless all the verified ones. ### Audit after v0.12.0 (2026-11-05): a crash, and what off really turns off

**A crash in v0.12.0 (`FastCombatStart`, default settings).** Every won combat is marked pre-finished, and a run continued from it enters that room again without ever reaching `StartTurn`: the window stayed open until the next combat, so every `Cmd.Wait` outside combat was instant. `NTransformPreview.CycleThroughCards` loops on `Cmd.Wait` (only its Instant branch uses `Task.Delay`): with a wait that completes at once it never gives the frame back. `audit-finished-room-on` (Continue, then Aroma of Chaos' transform screen): 0xC0000005, 3 of 3 sessions; `-fastoff` and `-control` went through. Now no window for a pre-finished room (counter 0 outside combat, public and beta), and `CombatManager.Reset` closes one left open (leaving during a combat start: read in `RunManager`'s cleanup, not provoked in game: a combat start with waits skipped is over within the command that starts it). `PunchOff` has the same kind of loop on v0.107.1 and v0.111; v0.113 calls `Cmd.TryWait` in both, which ends the loop when the wait completed at once (the preview would stop cycling instead of freezing; no game caller opens it during a combat). `MaxSkipsPerFrame` stays for mods' loops.

Also fixed:
- `SnapshotVerifier` said every snapshot optimization was off after a difference, but left `RelicStateReuse` on (`audit-verifier-relics`: its counter went on, 3 -> 9): `Settings.ApplyAll()` there.
- `listeners off` still left `HookSkip` on this class's mirrors and cached entries (`audit-listeners-off`: 1,578 -> 3,766): filtered walks are then the game's own walk through `KeptOnly`. In that mode no snapshot records a `MultiEnchantment`'s inner types, so `Keeps` notes them as with `_tracking` off, else a stacked enchantment would be dropped from hooks it reacts to (`audit-listeners-ab` matches `ab-2k-off`).
- `FastListeners.PilesKnown` is false after a fault: `PatchGuard` may have removed what bumps `PileVersion`, and `DeckCountMemo` would have answered from a frozen count (`audit-deckcount-fault`).
- `gc_pacing off` during a no-GC region left `SustainedLowLatency` (`audit-gc-latency`): the mode is set again when the region ends.

Every switch flipped in the middle of a combat, every cached state compared (`audit-toggles-2k`, solo) and each co-op peer flipping its own (`audit-cooptoggle`): results identical, no divergence.

Left for after v0.12.1, done in the section below ("The audit's other leads").

**Release checks v0.12.1 (2026-11-05).** `check_versions`: installed and published game (public and beta), BaseLib and Limitless all the verified ones. Both copies, Debug build, no baselines: `release-checks`, `effects-check` 53/56 as in v0.12.0, `ab-2k` 54, `pile-cards-2k` 131, `shuffle` 55, `combats3-2k` 63, `hookskip-off/on` 54, `chemical-x-big` 23, `deckcount` 131, `outside-2k` 48, `reshuffle-2k` 19, `autoslay-2k` 613 (public) and 387 (beta), `memory-runs` 32, `guard-async-ref/on` 19 lines identical, `audit-toggles-2k` and `audit-listeners-ab` identical to `ab-2k-off`, `audit-deckcount-fault` to `deckcount-off`; `snapcheck-hooks`, `-purehooks`, `-freeturn`, `-apotheosis`, `-5k`, `-10k`, `relics-verify-0..3`, `clonememo-check`, `solo-8k`, `fast-start-check`, `guard-checks`, `conflict-check`, `chemical-x` no problem; sweeps: relics 4,455 / 4,485, potions 328 / 333, encounters 3,146 / 3,321, cards 3,452 / 3,555, powers 5,240 / 5,342 lines identical (public / beta), `-Speed fast` relics on public identical; `coop`, `coopfast`, `coopclone`, `outside-coop`, `outside-coopoff`, `coopleak`, `snapcheckcoop`, `audit-cooptoggle` peers agree on both, `coop4`, `coop4leak` four peers agree on both; leaks: `memory-on` flat (public 172 -> 166 MB, beta 186 -> 180 MB over 15 turns at 8,400 cards), `memory-runs-on` no static of the mod reaches a left run, the positive control reached; `settings.ps1`, `baselib-optional.ps1`, `limitless-switches.ps1` all ok, `coop-required.ps1` refused on both, `menu-languages` ran on both, its captures not looked at. ### The audit's other leads (2026-11-05, after v0.12.1, not released)

- **Other mods on the walks.** Filtered walks ran `FastListeners`' mirror whatever another mod had patched on `IterateHookListeners`, and nothing looked at the iterators' state machines, `Contains` or the subscriber walk. All of them are now checked (`CombatWalkPatched`, `RunWalkPatched`), and a filtered walk then goes through the game's own (`KeptOnly`, which notes stacked enchantments itself: no snapshot does on that path). `audit-walk-conflict`, conflict on the combat walk: the cached entries' counter 45,085 -> 4,423 (what is left is the deck's, the run walk having no conflict), `listeners` and `hook_skip` now say "partly vanilla".
- **Parts of an option.** An option can declare `parts`, `setParts` and `wholeFaults`: its main patch's fault or absence no longer takes parts that don't depend on it, at the next `ApplyAll` or at all (`GcPacing`'s parts, `ControllerActionSkip`, `TextPoolReuse`...); only a snapshot that differed takes the whole of `coop_checksum`. An option is unavailable only when nothing of it applied, so less than 6 GB no longer reads "game code changed". `bdopt` lists what doesn't run (`partly off: DeckCountMemo (error)`, `audit-parts-visible`), the menu says so in the hover text (`parts_off`, 13 languages), and a part's conflicts count as its option's. The start of the log no longer says Limitless and BaseLib options are "not applied" while patches still apply.
- **The minimum on an update.** Each card of `DeckCountMemo` on its own, found by what its lambda returns (no compiler number in a name); `ExtraScopes`' BetterSpire2 scope doesn't need `SetupPlayerTurn`; each forwarded hook of `LimitlessHookSkip`; a hook or default of `HookFilter` gone or overloaded only takes that hook; `RelicFlashMerge`'s two handlers; `FastCombatStart`'s two waits. Mutations 38/38: `renumber-cards` (nothing left vanilla), `hook-rename`, `setup-turn`, `relic-holder` added, `wait-body` and `limitless-forward` may no longer lose their effect.
- **Back-offs.** `PileIndex`' getter (rechecked every 1,024 calls), `PileCounterBatch`, `CardDbScan`'s walk. None for `FastCombatStart`, on purpose: Limitless and SpeedX patch `Cmd.Wait`, a wait holds no logic, and the combat start must stay fast in co-op.
- **Small.** `PooledCardRelease` builds its handlers again only when one is freed, `CardDbScan` no closure with nothing pending, `HoverTipReuse` one `RemoveDupes`. A patch target not found is an update in the log, not an error. `package.py`: SteamCMD ends a value at a double quote, escaped or not (v0.12.1's change note was cut there, fixed by hand on the page).
- **Version** at the top right of the mod's page in BaseLib's menu.

Cost: `hookskip-on`, 8,400 cards, rendered, 4 sessions each alternating v0.12.1 and this build (DLL hash checked before each): plays 223 / 223 fps, 1% low median 50 / 53, worst frame median 19 / 19 ms; end of turn 239 / 239 fps, 1% low median 154 / 157. A first run of this comparison was void: the old build had failed to compile (a worktree needs an absolute `GameDataDir`) and all six sessions ran the new one, which only a hash check showed.

Checks, both copies: `ab-2k`, `pile-cards-2k`, `shuffle`, `combats3-2k`, `hookskip`, `chemical-x-big`, `deckcount`, `outside-2k`, `reshuffle-2k`, `autoslay-2k`, `memory-runs` identical, `snapcheck-hooks`, `-purehooks`, `-5k`, `relics-verify-0`, the `audit-*` scenarios no problem; `coop`, `coopfast`, `coopclone`, `outside-coop`, `coopleak`, `snapcheckcoop`, `audit-cooptoggle`, `coop4` peers agree; `settings.ps1`, `baselib-optional.ps1`, `limitless-switches.ps1` ok. Whole-game sweeps, the 8,400-card A/Bs and the Release package not rerun: for the next release.

### Last audit before V1 (2026-11-05 evening)

What the first audit and the leads after it hadn't covered: the code written since, read again; `MethodFingerprint`, `EquivalentFingerprints`, `HookSkipTable` (never read before); fallback paths only ever proven by reading.

- **`instant_combats` and loops on waits.** The v0.12.1 crash came from a loop pacing itself on `Cmd.Wait`; the option makes every wait of a combat instant, so a loop of that kind in combat (the game's, a mod's) froze the same way. More than 2,000 waits skipped in one frame (`MaxSkipsPerFrame`, about 20 times a whole combat start at 368 relics) is now a loop: that wait really waits, the next frame starts over. `bdperf waitspin` (`audit-waitspin`, co-op with `-Plain`: Limitless's own patch on `Cmd.Wait` yields a frame first in combat, which hides the loop): 2,001 waits at once, then the frame given back, public and beta.
- **`GcPacing.End`**: `EndNoGCRegion` throws if another thread used up the budget between the mode check and the call: caught, counted as exhausted. Read only, not provoked.
- **Back-offs, proven.** `audit-conflict-ab`: another mod on the two walks, both `Contains`, the subscriber walk, `CardModel.Pile`, `NCombatCardPile.RemoveCard` and `NetCombatCardDb.OnPileContentsChanged` at once, through the whole of `ab-2k`: 54 lines identical to `ab-2k-off` on public and beta, `CardDbScan`, `PileCounterBatch` and the cached entries at 0, `bdopt` naming the seven options partly vanilla.
- **Read, nothing found**: the fingerprint code (tokens, generated numbering, nested generated code, field writers) and the three equivalent fingerprints; `HookSkipTable.cs` regenerated from both versions' code (`fpdiff hookfps`, `classify.py`): the same file, 147 / 148 hooks safe, 0 unsafe; `fpdiff deps`: what this work added is pinned, but `CombatManager.Reset` (only closes the window) and `IHoverTip.RemoveDupes` (called as the game calls it).
- **Test tooling.** `parlib.ps1` counted a game that closed before its script's first line as done: `hookskip-on` at 8,400 cards did so once (8 s, nothing played) and `par.ps1`, comparing only `-off`/`-on` pairs of one call, never compared it with `turns-off`. Such a session now starts over. `waitfor room` took the room a peer left for the one asked when both were shops (the map had drawn one): it now waits for a room other than the one current at the peer's `cmd room` / `cmd event` (`outside-coop` and `-off` stopped there, mod on and off alike).
