- **Load check** (`PatchSafety.RequireResolvable`, ~25 ms for all patches): before applying a patch, `ModEntry.TryApply` resolves every member and type its class uses (nested types, lambdas and state machines included) and checks they are accessible. A member removed, changed or made private skips that patch (vanilla). `RuntimeHelpers.PrepareMethod` would also catch other JIT failures, but methods compiled that way skip tiered compilation: turns at 32k cards were ~2% slower.
- **Rollback** (`PatchGuard`): an Apply that fails halfway has the patches it already installed removed.
- **Runtime guard** (`PatchGuard`): every patched method gets a void finalizer (inlined null check, no measurable cost). Harmony 2.4 rethrows an exception unchanged only when every finalizer on the method, from any mod, is void; one returning an `Exception` makes it throw again, its trace then starting at the replacement method (the game's or another mod's frames lost, even with `bdopt off`: patches stay installed). All pass-through finalizers of this mod are void since the fourth 2026-11-15 audit pass (25 returned the exception); only `HookFilter.RethrowFinalizer` (7 mirrored hooks) returns one, because it substitutes an exception, and it logs the full trace first. An exception leaving a patched method through a frame of a patch class faults that patch (off for the session, menu says so) and removes all its Harmony patches on the next frame. Test: `faultprobe` (scenario `guard-checks`). It can't see a patch method the JIT refuses to compile (the exception comes from the replacement method, with no frame of the patch class): that case is the load check's (`PatchSafety.RequireResolvable` resolves every member a patch class uses before it applies; `[PatchPart]` methods, another game version's code, are checked before their part is used) and each prefix's own try/catch (an access failure is thrown at the call, inside it).
- **Fingerprints of what each patch relies on** (v0.7.0, `MethodFingerprint`): IL with tokens resolved to names, the compiler's numbering of generated members ignored (`<Hook>d__42`, display classes, lambdas: it shifts when the game adds a member above, with the same code), and the code of the generated methods it uses included (lambdas, local functions, state machines: a changed lambda changes its user's fingerprint). Besides the mirrored methods themselves, patches pin what their reasoning assumes: HookSkip and HookFilter the `AbstractModel` defaults they skip (a default that stops being a no-op leaves its hooks vanilla), HookSkip the choice-context helpers (`HookPlayerChoiceContext` constructor/`GetOwner`/`AssignTaskAndWaitForPauseOrCompletion`/`WaitForCompletion`, `PushModel`/`PopModel`, `InvokeExecutionFinished`) for the hooks using them. Patches whose caches are kept up to date by watching some methods check that no other code may change what those methods guard (`PatchSafety.RequireFieldWriters`: assignments, mutating calls, the collection passed to a method taking a mutable one; new readers don't count; a new writer would change no fingerprint): `CardPile._cards` (ListenerCache, PileIndex, FastListeners), `Player._relics` and `_potionSlots` (ListenerCache), `AbstractModel.ExecutionFinished` (FastListeners' subscriber tracking). `tools/fpdiff fieldusers <dir> <type> <field> [--writers]` lists them. HookSkip also backs off a hook whose state machine another mod patches (a transpiler there could add a walk that this patch's redirect would filter), except BaseLib's AfterEnergyReset and AfterCardPlayed transpilers (3.4.7 and 3.4.8, the same) (they only make the hook first await a method of BaseLib's own; transpilers and their injector `AsyncMethodCall.Create` fingerprinted) and BaseLib 3.4.8's prefix and transpiler on `Hook.ModifyBlock` (they apply the card's own modifiers to the block before the walks; patches and the two methods the inserted code calls fingerprinted). Until v0.6.4 this wasn't checked: those two BaseLib transpilers ran with the redirect (harmless, see above), any other would have gone unnoticed. A fingerprint from another game version is accepted when that code was checked to behave the same, so one DLL keeps the patch on both: per version where a generator re-validates it (HookSkip's table: `tools/hookskip/classify.py` classifies every supported version on its own code), by hand otherwise (`EquivalentFingerprints.cs`, each entry saying what changed: beta v0.111 `CardPile.RemoveInternal`, `SavedProperties.FromInternal`, `SetupPlayerTurn`; beta v0.113 `CardPile.AddInternal`, `Hook.IterateCombatHookListeners`, `NCardFlyShuffleVfx.PlayAnim`, `NHoverTipSet.Init`, `CheckMouseDragThreshold`, `CardModel.DeepCloneFields`, `AfterCloned`, `UpgradeInternal`; BaseLib 3.4.8's heal hooks; HookSkip's own table compares exactly, never through these equivalents), or by a mirror per version chosen from the fingerprint (FastListeners' walks, HookFilter's damage hook). `tools/fpdiff review <old> <new>` lists every fingerprinted method that changed between two versions with its IL and C# diff, the members it newly calls (with their code, and whether they changed) and renames; `tools/autotest/mutations.ps1` checks the reactions in game. Beta v0.111 with v0.7.0: nothing left vanilla (147 hooks), A/B `ab-2k-off/on` identical on both versions.
- **Errors inside async hooks** (v0.7.0): most hooks run in async state machines, where an exception only faults the hook's task and never leaves the patched method, so `PatchGuard` can't see it. The code of this mod running there falls back by itself: FastListeners' own pile-entry cache, on an exception, turns FastListeners off (HookSkip with it: its option lists both faults) and the walk adds every pile entry like vanilla (a superset whose extra entries keep the no-op defaults), so the hook completes normally; HookSkip's per-call checks turn HookSkip off and walk vanilla. Test: `bdperf fault piles`, scenarios `guard-async-on` vs `guard-async-ref` (same checksums). A method, field or type missing at load is logged as a skipped patch (update), not an error.
- **Harmony rebuilds** (`HarmonyRebuild`, v1.0.1): every Patch and Unpatch of this mod (`PatchGuard`) and every `InlineGuard` recompile builds the method's replacement again from all its patches, every mod's. Harmony refinds a stored patch method by module and token, which for one declared in a generic type gives the open generic method: that rebuild can't compile. And Harmony 2.4 undoes the method's detour before making the new one, so a failed rebuild left the method with no mod's patches at all (v1.0.0, see "Harmony rebuilds that dropped another mod's patches"). Now a rebuild is refused before anything is touched when a patch on the method can't be refound (`Blocker`) or carries a transpiler known to keep state between its runs (`StatefulTranspilers`: BaseLib 3.4.8's `ModInterop+QuickTranspiler` inserts a static list it reassigns before each `[ModInterop]` stub is patched, so a later rerun would give a stub the last stub's call; no installed mod uses `[ModInterop]`; InlineGuard's reach check doesn't rerun such a transpiler either), and a rebuild that fails anyway gets the previous replacement back (`PatchTools.detours` and `DetourMethod`, read by reflection and checked at load; without them `InlineGuard` rebuilds nothing). After the patches are applied, `HarmonyRebuild: all N patched methods detoured` is logged, or an error naming each method left without its patches. Tests: `rebuild-check` (also with `BDTEST_REBUILD_ALL=1`: every patched method rebuilt), `rebuild-control` (v1.0.0's way: must fail).
- **Off at start** (`ModEntry.StartedOff`): when BaseLib's saved settings have the whole mod off when the game starts, no patch is applied but `CoopModeTag` (and the test tooling): switching it off and restarting always gives the unmodified game, whatever the bug. Turning it back on then takes effect at the next start (bdopt and the menu say so). Scenario `started-off` (run with `"Enabled": "False"` in the cfg).

| Patch | What | Scope / safety |
|---|---|---|
| `ListenerCache` | Materializes `CombatState.IterateHookListeners()` once and replays it | Only inside scopes: **snapshot** (`NetFullCombatState.FromRun`) and **refresh** (`PlayerCombatState.RecalculateCardValues`; the whole `CombatStateChanged` dispatch via a transpiler on `CombatStateTracker.CallCombatStateChangedDeferred` → one scope for hand, end turn button, intents, creature state, energy, orbs; `NHandCardHolder.UpdateCard`, `NCard.UpdateVisuals` when called outside it). Also caches `RunState.IterateHookListeners` (deck + combat). Invalidated (finalizers, so a watched method that throws after its change still invalidates; `ListenerCache.Apply` has the list) on `CombatStateTracker.NotifyCombatStateChanged`, every `CardPile` list method (`AddInternal`, `RemoveInternal`, `MoveToBottom/TopInternal`, `RandomizeOrderInternal`, `Clear`), `Player.Add/RemoveRelicInternal`, `RelicModel.IsMelted`, `Player.IsActiveForHooks`, the potion methods, `OrbQueue.TryEnqueue/Remove/Insert/Clear`, the `CardModel.Enchantment`/`Affliction` setters, v0.113's dominion setters, `RunManager.CleanUp`, and (fourth audit pass, optional, each a watch of its own) `CombatState.AddCreature/RemoveCreature/SortEnemiesBySlotName/SetEnemyIndex` and `Creature.ApplyPowerInternal/RemovePowerInternal`; field writers of `CardPile._cards`, `Player._relics`/`_potionSlots`, `OrbQueue._orbs` and the setters' fields pinned. A hand selection ended by a state change resumes the card's play inline in the dispatch scope: `NPlayerHand.RevalidateSelectionAfterStateChange` suspends the caches. Thread-static state; scope depth stored as depth + 1 in `__state` (0: our prefix never ran, nothing closed). `_safe` also needs `Apply` to have finished. Backs off while another mod patches the walks' code (`FastListeners.WalkCodePatchedByOthers`: iterators, `Contains`, ModHelper's subscriber walks). |
| `HookFilter` | Inside any `ListenerCache` scope (snapshot or refresh), `Hook.ModifyEnergyCostInCombat`, `Hook.ModifyKeywordsInCombat`, `Hook.ShouldPlay`, `Hook.ShouldAllowHitting`, `Hook.ModifyDamageInternal` (additive/multiplicative/cap, and v0.113's `ModifyDamageMultiplicativeLate`) and the cost colors' own copies of the cost loops (`CardCostHelper.TryModifyEnergyCostWithHooks` / `TryModifyStarCostWithHooks`, fingerprinted, base methods return false with the cost unchanged) only call listeners whose type overrides the `AbstractModel` hook (base = no-op: +0, ×1m, cap MaxValue, true), same order. Lists built from FastListeners' filtered walk per hook (cached per scope and version), or from the materialized list when FastListeners is off | Mirrors the vanilla hook bodies; each hook fingerprinted (a changed hook stays vanilla), each skipped `AbstractModel` default too (a default that changed leaves its hook vanilla). Damage: one mirror per signature (v0.107 / v0.111's extra `CardPlay`), listener methods called through delegates made at load, so the same class loads on both. Each mirror lives in its own class with its own load check (`HookFilterEnergyCost`, `...Keywords`, `...ShouldPlay`, `...Hitting`, `...CostColors`, `...Damage`): a game member one of them uses that changes leaves only that hook vanilla. Backs off per hook if another mod patches the hook or its base method (BaseLib patches `Hook.ModifyDamage`, not `ModifyDamageInternal`); only the hooks whose game code walks `Hook.IterateCombatHookListeners` (energy cost, ShouldPlay, hitting) also back off when that walk or the combat getters it reads are patched (keywords, damage and star cost walk the listeners directly). The energy cost, ShouldPlay and hitting mirrors take the game's early exits (negative cost, combat ending) before building a list. A listener's own exception is rethrown as vanilla would (`RethrowFinalizer`: its trace reset, logged in full first). |
| `SnapshotVerifier` | Live safety net for the checksum: first snapshot of the session (plus the next two while cheap), then 1 in 25 within a 1% play-time budget, rebuilt vanilla and compared byte for byte. Skipped above 1,000 combat cards or after a re-check over 1 s (a vanilla rebuild freezes the game for seconds at that size) | On mismatch: vanilla snapshot is used, snapshot optimizations off for the session, error logged. |
| `IdleButtonInput` | Input processing off for idle `NButton`s whose `_Input` is NButton's own (drag-cancel), on while pressed: a prefix on Godot's `Node.SetProcessInput` (runs for every node; only eligible idle buttons are muted, every mute tracked), input back from the postfixes on `OnPressHandler`, `OnReleaseHandler`, `CheckMouseDragThreshold` and `ConnectSignals`; turned off, muted buttons get it back within 30 frames | UI only. `_Input` and the drag check pinned (v0.113's drag check equivalent); the writers of `NClickableControl._isPressed` pinned (`RequireFieldWriters`, same on the four versions): a new way of pressing leaves the patch off. |
| `GridSortCache` | Inside `NCardGrid.SetCards`, the sort comparers (obtained order, cost, title; asc/desc) answer from per-card values computed once: index map with `IndexOf`'s equality, resolved cost, culture `SortKey`s | Display order only. Each comparer is replaced only if its fingerprint matches the vanilla lambda; the cost and title values live in their own classes (`GridSortCost`, `GridSortTitle`, own load check): a changed cost or title API only leaves those orders vanilla. The index map is exact only while `SetCards` sorts `_cardsCache` and refills `_cards` after the sort: `SetCards` fingerprinted (`224B19A92FC91776`, four versions), a change leaves only the obtained orders vanilla. The `SortingAlgorithms` getter's postfix returns at once once the current `SetCards` call checked that dictionary. |
| `EnchantmentTabReuse` | Limitless only (applied when its assembly loads, skipped if absent): mirrors `CardExtensions.UpdateEnchantmentVisuals` but reuses the card's existing tabs instead of freeing and duplicating them | UI only. Off unless the mirrored method's fingerprint matches (Limitless v1.1.7 to v1.2.1, same code), with what the mirror relies on (`CardExtensions.SetEnchantmentStatus`, `TabStride`, `EnchantmentTabHost.EnsureAttached`, fingerprinted on 1.2.1); backs off if another mod patches it. A new tab is added to the host after it is configured, in Limitless's order; an exception before that frees it, then falls back. |
| `PoolSignalScan` | Prefix on non-generic `NodePool.Free(IPoolable)` mirroring `NodePool<T>.Free`: same walk, disconnects (pool's own `DisconnectSignal`) and bookkeeping, but signal names cached per (type, class, script) and connection lists read only when `HasConnections` | UI only. Fingerprints of the 4 mirrored methods required (static `Free`, `Free(T)`, the explicit `INodePool.Free`, the walk); only the game's constructed `NodePool<>` classes are mirrored (BaseLib's `GeneratedNodePool<T>` or a mod's own `INodePool` stays vanilla, judged per class before any member is read); another mod's patch on any pool's mirrored methods keeps every free vanilla; names re-checked vs vanilla for the first 3 nodes per type then 1 in 64, any difference turns it off. |
| `ListenerCache` reuse | Display-only refresh scopes (`NCard.UpdateVisuals`, `NHandCardHolder.UpdateCard`, BetterSpire2's recalculation) hand their listener lists to the next display scope while the invalidation version is unchanged | Never when a snapshot or game-state refresh scope (`RecalculateCardValues`, the CombatStateChanged dispatch, the first-turn filter) is or was open in the same outermost scope: those always build fresh lists, and lists taken from a kept one are dropped when such a scope opens inside. Kept lists dropped at every invalidation (they held a finished combat). Not while a mod defines a concrete BaseLib `CardModifier` (its subscriber's output changes with nothing watched; looked up only in the loaded "BaseLib" assembly). 1 reuse in 16 rebuilt and compared; a difference turns reuse off for the session (fault `ListenerCache.Reuse`). |
| `ExtraScopes` | Refresh scopes around `SetupPlayerTurn`'s two first-turn `ToList` (Innate / bottom-of-pile filters, transpiler) and BetterSpire2's `DamageTracker.Recalculate` (when that mod loads) | Read-only passes. SetupPlayerTurn state machine fingerprint required. |
| `PileIndex` | `CardModel.Pile` from a per-card membership index kept by `CardPile.AddInternal`/`RemoveInternal` prefixes (a `Priority.First` watch prefix takes the pile's count; after a throw or a skipped original the finalizer judges from the list: kept when the card really entered or left the pile, undone when it didn't, the index faulted when the card was moved again meanwhile or the pile changed unseen; a card mid-add or mid-remove while another mod patches those gets the game's scan, `InNoPile` answers unsure for it; `Clear` frames count their own removes and are judged once, a Harmony finalizer rerun skipped); one pile → that pile if in the owner's `Piles`, none → null, anything else → vanilla scan. Also: `AddInternal`'s duplicate check answers false when the index counted this add as the card's only pile (transpiler), `RunState.ContainsCard` searches `_allCards` from the end (fingerprinted, same answer; its own flag `ContainsCardEnabled`, so an index fault needn't bring the scan back once Settings sets it from `pile_lookup`), and `InNoPile` for FastListeners | Game logic: exact by construction (only CardPile's methods touch `_cards`; Clear failures and shuffle hooks that change the list disable it). 1 answer in 1024 compared with the vanilla scan (Debug: `bdperf pile verify 1` = all). Fingerprints of the getter and every list-touching CardPile method. |
| `CardDbScan` | Skips `NetCombatCardDb`'s whole-pile walk when every card added to that pile since its last walk already has an ID (the walk would ID nothing) | Network IDs: same cards, same order (vanilla walk runs whenever something needs an ID; ownerless cards stay pending). Only piles the database itself IDed at combat start or walked since are tracked (v0.7.0: no assumption on which pile types it watches; an untracked pile gets a vanilla walk first). Fingerprints: the walk (`OnPileContentsChanged`), `IdCardIfNecessary`, `StartCombat` (the same on the four versions); field writers of `CardPile._cards` and `NetCombatCardDb._cardToId`/`_nextId` pinned. `AddInternal` deliberately not fingerprinted (its body changed on v0.113): it only has to be the only way into a pile, and an add storing another object is caught by the 1-in-64 whole-pile check. Walks are the game's while another mod patches `AddInternal` (until the next combat start). |
| `PileCounterBatch` | Inside `CardPile.Clear`, pile buttons update their count like vanilla but fit the label once at the end | UI only. `NCombatCardPile.RemoveCard` fingerprint. The clear depth after opening goes through `__state` and the finalizer restores it, so Harmony's rerun of every finalizer (one of them throwing) can't leave it negative. |
| `FastCombatStart` | Visual concession: while the combat loads, from entering a combat room (not a pre-finished one) until its first turn starts or the combat is reset (prefix on `CombatManager.StartTurn`: room entry and "start of combat" effects only; the first turn's start-of-turn effects, draw and everything after keep the game's pace), `Cmd.Wait`/`CustomScaledWait` return at once (like the game's Instant speed). Until v0.6.1 the window only closed on a wait during the local player's play phase, which in solo often never came (the game sets every phase to `None` outside the player's turn): whole combats ran without waits; scenarios `pace-on/off/vanilla/instant` time the turns (identical to vanilla). Option `instant_combats` (v0.6.2, off by default): every wait of every combat instant, this player only. Applies in solo from the relic threshold (all players' relics; setting `fast_combat_start_relics`, 0–100 in steps of 10 (the slider's Godot Range snaps other values and saves the snapped one, so bdopt and loading round the same way), default 0 = always; it was a fixed 50 until v0.5.0) | Waits never change state (each player already has their own speed); verified identical relic results in singleplayer (fast / normal / mod off) and in local co-op with 368 relics and the Workshop mods (both peers identical every turn, ~105 waits = 72–79 s skipped per combat start). Option `fast_combat_start` and its threshold, solo only: always on in co-op whatever the relics (master switch and faults still apply), so no modded player starts ~70 s after the others; the BaseLib menu shows a note about it at the top (which also says that the whole mod can't be turned off during a co-op run without confirming, see `bdopt`). A wait that is no longer async is left to the game alone. `SkippedWaits`/`SkippedSeconds` count only waits the game would really have made (not its non-interactive mode, not at Instant speed, a positive time). |
| `Settings` / `SettingsMenu` / `BaseLibSettings` | Master switch + one switch per optimization (effective = master && option && not faulted, `PatchSafety.Fault`). With BaseLib (optional), shown in its mod configuration (`SimpleModConfig`, one static bool property per option, saved in `mod_configs/BigDeckOptimization.cfg`, bdopt changes saved too; menu texts merged into the `settings_ui` table at runtime, no pck, English and the game's 13 other languages, 56 keys each). `BaseLibSettings` lives in `src/BigDeckOptimization.Menu` (own DLL, built by the main project's `BuildMenu` target): the game calls `GetTypes()` on a mod's DLL, so a type deriving from a BaseLib class made the whole mod fail when BaseLib wasn't loaded yet or at all. `SettingsMenu` loads that DLL in the mod's load context once BaseLib is `Loaded` (now or via `ModManager.OnModDetected`: mods load in the player's order). A switch whose patch can't run (`Settings.Why`: Limitless missing or changed, game code changed or the patch failed to apply (`ModEntry.NotApplied`), faulted) is greyed out and not clickable, its hover text starting with the reason (`BaseLibSettings.SetupConfigUI`, texts rebuilt each time the page opens). Limitless-only switches (`limitless_tabs`, `limitless_saves`): the box shows ticked only while the patch is applied and wanted; the wanted state is saved in hidden properties (`LimitlessTabsWanted`, `LimitlessSavesWanted`), so a box shown unticked without Limitless comes back ticked once it is installed (the shown values are ignored when loading and excluded from Restore defaults). During a co-op run (`Settings.InCoopRun`: a run in progress, not singleplayer) the master switch can't be unticked (greyed, its hover text giving `why.coop_run`); the fast-start relic slider is greyed, without input, whenever `fast_combat_start` itself is (started off, faulted, unavailable), with that option's reason | Without BaseLib: all patches, `bdopt` session-only. Tested: this mod before BaseLib (menu registers when BaseLib loads), BaseLib disabled, normal order (save + reload). |
| Packaging | DLLs built with `DebugType=embedded`: the portable PDB is compressed inside each DLL (line numbers kept in stack traces, no `.pdb` files shipped): 232 -> 206 KB for both DLLs + PDBs. Release paths mapped to `/_/` | Mod folder ~444 KB (v0.3.0: 642 KB). |
| `ModImage` | Mods screen: shows `mod_image.jpg` from the mod folder for this mod (the game only looks in a pck). JPEG quality 97 with full-resolution color: 242 KB instead of the 433 KB PNG (max error 14/255, PSNR 42.5 dB; lossy WebP tops out at ~33 dB even at q100, its color is always half resolution) | UI only. Follows the master switch and its fault like the other patches (`bdopt off`: the game's empty image); an image Godot can't decode is logged once and not read again. |
| `FastListeners` | Uncached `CombatState`/`RunState.IterateHookListeners` (outside ListenerCache scopes): same code and laziness (list built on first MoveNext, Contains checked at yield, mod subscribers lazily), pooled list, cards tested first. BaseLib's "BaseLibCardModifiers" subscriber is skipped while it can only return an empty list (its lambda's fingerprint matches and no concrete CardModifier subclass is loaded; rechecked when an assembly referencing BaseLib loads). Provides the filtered walks used by `HookSkip`, with a per-filter cache of the kept pile entries (per PlayerCombatState / deck) invalidated by `PileVersion`, bumped before and after every CardPile list method (Add/Remove/MoveToTop/MoveToBottom/RandomizeOrder/Clear, fingerprinted) and every Enchantment/Affliction setter. Filters walk a shared `PileSnapshot` (every pile entry in walk order with a small type id, built once per `PileVersion`): one array test per entry instead of a type lookup. Appends at the end of a pile extend the snapshot and segments instead of rebuilding them (journal of consecutive appends); enchantment/affliction changes of a card in no pile don't bump. Filters tracking ExecutionFinished subscribers are cached per `SubscriptionVersion` (bumped around the event's accessors and the hand's select-mode methods). Main thread only, and never inside `RandomizeOrderInternal` (shuffle listeners reorder the pile list in place). Appends have their own switch (`_appendMoves`: off with a changed `AddInternal` or a move-check mismatch, which can't tell an append from a shift). An exception in this mod's verdicts inside a filtered walk (kinds, relics, per-entry judgments, the per-version tails) turns FastListeners off and refills the walk unfiltered (the vanilla superset), as the pile entry cache always did. The mirrored mod-subscriber walk throws the game's "Collection was modified" exception when a subscription is added mid-walk, as the game's `foreach` does | `bdperf listeners`: same objects in the same order as vanilla. Debug builds compare 1 cache hit in 256 with a fresh walk (`bdperf segments verify all\|sample\|off`, against an independent reference: piles read live, each entry judged by the filter's predicate, no type-id table or cached verdict); every build checks a subscriber-filter hit against live subscribers at most once every 2 s (difference: those filters uncached for the session). |
| `HookSkip` | All `Hook` methods walking listeners: 147 on v0.107.1, 154 on v0.113.x (v0.111.0's rows dropped 2026-11-16: its hooks run the game's code) (`tools/hookskip/classify.py` checks, on each supported game version's own code, that each loop only does no-ops for a listener keeping the `AbstractModel` defaults of the methods it calls; table generated into `HookSkipTable.cs`: per version, the hook's fingerprint, the fingerprint of each default it relies on, and for hooks using choice contexts that version's helper fingerprints; a hook runs filtered only when one version's entry matches all of them): a transpiler redirects their walk calls to a walk minus the pile entries (cards, affliction, enchantment, deck cards) whose type overrides none of those methods; entries with ExecutionFinished subscribers kept where the hook signals them (judged when the walk's list is built: an entry getting its first subscriber during the walk, before its turn, isn't signalled by that walk; the only subscriber, the hand's card selection, moves hand nodes and is consumed by the source's own later walks first: accepted, UI only). For hooks keeping no subscriber, listeners outside piles are left out too (the "FastListeners kinds" row, same option). BaseLib's hand size, heal and downgrade hooks: their walk keeps only implementers of their interface (a pending run filter BaseLib's walk didn't take, NullRunState walking the combat directly, is dropped by a first-priority prefix on `CombatState.IterateHookListeners`). A hook's fingerprint must equal its table row exactly (not through `EquivalentFingerprints`); a game `Hook` method with no row that walks the listeners is counted and logged as left vanilla. Also inside `ListenerCache` scopes (after v0.3.0: the walk reads the same unchanged state without materializing every listener); inside ListenerCache scopes, `HookFilter`'s mirrors (`ShouldAllowHitting` among them, BetterSpire2's preview) build each hook's list from FastListeners' filtered walk, cached per scope, listener version and pile version (from the materialized list when FastListeners is off); outside scopes the game's hook runs with HookSkip's filtered walk. The transpiler runs at `Priority.Last` (other mods' transpilers registered at load see the original walk calls; Harmony orders equal priorities by registration, so one at priority 0 or less registered after HookSkip's deferred batches sees the redirect calls: HookSkip then backs off that hook, but that mod's matcher may miss its anchor; no mod does this). The choice-context helpers' state machines (`MoveNext`) are checked for other mods' patches like the helpers themselves | Per hook: changed code or another mod patching the hook/base method = vanilla for that hook only. Per type: overriders are always walked. Verified: full combat checksums identical on/off over 5 turns + combat end (`hookskip-on/off`), no leak over 15 turns (`memory-on/off`). At 8,400 cards: turn 3.92 -> 3.01 s, Hidden Gem turn 10.4 -> 3.6 s. |
| `HoverTipReuse` | While the mouse is on a creature, its hover tips are rebuilt after a state change only when the computed tips differ (all fields), the panel is gone, or what the game places it from changed (the hitbox's global position, size and scale, the viewport size: a creature moved or resized, Osty growing); backs off on other mods' patches to `NHoverTipSet`'s build, placement and removal (v0.113's split methods included), each pinned per version; while `NHoverTipSet.shouldBlockHoverTips` (potion popup, targeting) or `NGame.IsDebugHidingHoverTips` is set, the game's rebuild runs (it removes the panel) | UI only; `hover-tips-8k` checks the panel matches the current tips after each phase. |
| `MethodFingerprint` | Helper: hash of a method's IL with tokens resolved to member names (stable across recompiles), compiler numbering of generated members ignored, generated code it uses (lambdas, state machines) included (v0.7.0) | Patches that mirror code require the fingerprint of the code they mirror, so updates only disable them when that code changes. |
| `PatchSafety` | Helper: is a method Harmony-patched by another owner? (optionally ignoring listed postfixes of other mods whose effect a patch reproduces, or, at call sites where that is exact, patches of a shape that can't change what the patch relies on: `PatchTolerance`, "Foreign patches judged by their shape"). Answers cached 2 s, read without a lock | — |
| `RelicFlashMerge` | A relic flashing again in the same frame shows one flash: `NRelicInventoryHolder.OnRelicFlashed` (particles over the relic bar) once per holder per frame, `Player.OnRelicFlashed` (sound + creature effect) once per relic and single target per frame | Sound and visuals only (fingerprints of both handlers and `DoFlash`; backs off when another mod patches them). Option `relic_flashes`. |
| `TypePlaqueSkip` | BaseLib only: its `AddSubtypesToTypePlaquePatch.TryModifyPlaqueText` (run on every `NCard.UpdateTypePlaque`) walks every combat hook listener for `ICardTypeTextModifier`s; with no loaded type implementing that interface it returns the original text without the walk | UI text only. Fingerprint (BaseLib 3.4.7 and 3.4.8, identical); BaseLib's code runs for `ICustomTypeTextCard` cards, once a modifier type is loaded, or when another mod patches the method. `bdperf plaque`: same object for every card. Option `card_type_line`. |
| `LimitlessReserialize` | Limitless only: before `MultiEnchantment.SetSerializableCards` replaces its saved list of inner cards (after every forwarded call; `RecalculateValues` runs for every combat card at every state change), computes what the new list would hold with vanilla's own calls (`SavedProperties.From`, BaseLib's registered save getters) and compares it with the list in place: ids, upgrade level, floor, enchantment id/amount, saved properties (`SerializableCard`s by reference), BaseLib extended save data (read from its table without adding; expected data built in one reused data object by BaseLib's registered getters; values compared only when primitive/string/enum/empty collection; main thread, not re-entrant). All equal: the list in place stays (same content; its objects keep their BaseLib entries, nothing removed from any table). Otherwise Limitless runs | Checksummed state, identical content: `pile-cards-on/off` and `hookskip-on`/`turns-off` CHECKSUM/STATE lines match. Every build (Debug only before the fourth 2026-11-15 pass): 1 kept list in 4,096 serialized (packet bytes and bit length, BaseLib data included) against `cards.Select(ToSerializable)`, `SetSerializableCards`' own body; a difference turns it off for the session and faults it (SnapshotCardReuse and LimitlessRecalcSkip follow; Limitless rebuilds the list). The list shortcut (also SnapshotCardReuse's memo plan) only for a `[SavedProperty]` with `AlwaysSave` (another condition may leave an empty list out). Duplicate BaseLib save ids counted once. A late Limitless load applies only the patches not yet applied. Fingerprints: `SetSerializableCards` (Limitless 1.1.7 to 1.2.1), `CardModel`/`EnchantmentModel.ToSerializable` (v0.107.1 to v0.113.1), `SavedProperties.From`/`FromInternal`, BaseLib's two postfixes (3.4.7 to 3.4.8), `ExtendedSaveData` ctor and `DictForType`. Backs off when another mod patches any of these (BaseLib's own postfixes aside). |
| `LimitlessRecalcSkip` | Limitless only, with `LimitlessReserialize`: `PlayerCombatState.RecalculateCardValues` calls every combat card's enchantment `RecalculateValues` after every state change and after every affliction applied or removed (`AfflictionModel.Amount` setter, `ClearAfflictionInternal`): once per card over every card when Hex afflicts a deck (17 million `MultiEnchantment.RecalculateValues` at 4,100 cards). When `LimitlessReserialize`'s comparison keeps a MultiEnchantment's list, a memo records what it read: the saved list and the inner card list (same objects, same `List._version`), each inner card's upgrade, floor, enchantment (reference) and that enchantment's amount, BaseLib's registered save counts and `BaseLibTypes.Changes`. A later `RecalculateValues` on it returns at once while all of it holds and every inner enchantment type keeps the game's empty `RecalculateValues` and saves no property (then the inner calls are no-ops and `SetSerializableCards` would keep the list); the memo's values are re-read at each call. On top, whole passes: a `RecalculateCardValues` pass is skipped while it would be quiet, tracked through setters: prefixes on `EnchantmentModel.Amount`, `CardModel.CurrentUpgradeLevel`/`FloorAddedToDeck`/`Enchantment`, `MultiEnchantment.SerializableEnchantmentsOnCards`/`AddEnchantment`/`DeepCloneFields` and a finalizer on `CardPile.AddInternal` bump a quiet counter (main-thread bumps in its low 32 bits, other threads' in the high bits: a pass seeing only an off-main bump with nothing changed is not a mismatch). This relies on InlineGuard (a setter inlined into a caller would be missed) and on `RequireFieldWriters` at load (the saved list's backing field written only by `.ctor` and its setter, its private getter called only by `get_EnchantmentsOnCards` and `DeepCloneFields`: else only pass skipping stays off). One pass in 64 runs for real and is checked: a memo not holding then means a change went unseen, and pass skipping stops for the session | Checksummed state: `party5-on/off` CHECKSUM/STATE identical (190 lines). One skip in 1,024 re-runs the full comparison (release builds too): a difference or an exception turns it off for the session (the rest of `limitless_saves` keeps working). Fingerprints: `MultiEnchantment.RecalculateValues`, the `EnchantmentsOnCards` getter and `DeepCloneFields` (Limitless 1.1.7 to 1.2.1), `EnchantmentModel.RecalculateValues` (empty), `PlayerCombatState.RecalculateCardValues`, `AllCards` (v0.107.1 to v0.113.1). Needs `LimitlessReserialize` applied (its prefix fills the memo); backs off with it or when another mod patches these (rechecked every 1,024 calls, no clock read per card; `AllPiles` gets no back-off: BaseLib transpiles it, and quiet passes read the same getter). |
| `SnapshotCardReuse` | With `LimitlessReserialize` (BaseLib + Limitless): the co-op checksum after every action (`NetFullCombatState.FromRun`, also in solo on the v0.111 beta) builds `card.ToSerializable()` for every combat card, with BaseLib's extended save data (a ConditionalWeakTable entry per new SerializableCard / SerializableEnchantment). A transpiler replaces only that call in `NetFullCombatState.CardState.From` (exactly one call, else skipped; the rest of `From` stays the game's): the card's previous result (kept per card) is used again when `LimitlessReserialize`'s field-by-field comparison says `ToSerializable` would hold the same; otherwise the game's call, and its result becomes the kept one. The snapshot only serializes and keeps it (printed on a divergence), never changes it. Step S: a pile with written cards reads only those (each write marks its card in the pile it was last read in: write-marking prefixes on `CardEnergyCost` and the other written members), and piles stay trusted while only known pure cost or keyword listeners stand with the same amounts (a prefix on `CombatPileState.From`). Card bits (a prefix on `CardState.Serialize`) and pile bits (a transpiler replacing `PlayerState.Serialize`'s one `WriteList<CombatPileState>` call, checked to be exactly one before and after patching) reuse what was written; their back-off list includes `WriteModelEntry` and `WriteInt`, which the upgrade bits write into. `Player.AfterCombatEnd` releases the combat's entries. A cost or keyword listener throwing inside the calls the game's `From` also makes lets the game's `From` run (that pile untrusted, `GameExceptions`), so the game's own error doesn't fault the part; an error in `card.ToSerializable()` still does. | Checksummed: `party5-on/off` identical (the `checksum` command uses `FromRun`); co-op `party5coop`: no divergence. One reuse in 2,048 is serialized next to a fresh `ToSerializable` (release builds too): a bit-length or byte difference or an exception turns it off for the session (fresh objects from then on; the rest of `coop_checksum` keeps working). Off if `LimitlessReserialize` faulted or another mod patches what its comparison mirrors. |
| `TextPoolReuse` | SmartFormat 3.3 (the game's text formatting, templates parsed on every format): `Placeholder.FormatterOptions` creates a new `ArrayPool<char>` (`ArrayPool.Create(1024, 1024)`, ~56 KB of bucket arrays) for each new placeholder to rent one buffer. A transpiler returns `ArrayPool<char>.Shared` from that call instead; the buffer only receives the unescaped options from its start, then is copied into the cached string | Display only: `bdperf text` formats every card's description (enchantment and affliction texts included) both ways: 8,260 texts identical, 509 -> 60 MB allocated, 211 -> 75 ms. Fingerprints: the getter and `EscapedLiteral.UnEscapeCharLiterals` (the shared pool's buffers may hold old characters where a new pool's are zeroed: correct because that method writes from index 0 and returns only what it wrote); SmartFormat 3.3.0, `SmartFormat.dll` byte-identical on the four game versions. Part of `combat_refresh`. |
| `InlineGuard` | Helper: every patched original has the runtime's no-inlining flag (`mdfNotInline`), so a caller compiled later (tier 1) can't inline its original body past the patch. Harmony 2.4's MonoMod.Core already sets it on every detoured method (`PlatformTripleDetourFactory.CreateDetour` -> `FxCoreBaseRuntime.DisableInlining`, same word and bit), so the flag write is redundant; what InlineGuard adds is the recompile: once every patch is in (Step S), each Harmony-patched method whose code calls a protected one (directly or through callees up to 4 levels deep, memoized per depth) is rebuilt by Harmony with its same patches: replacements other mods had compiled before had the bodies inlined (BaseLib's `SetToFreeThisTurn`: Bullet Time's cost writes went unseen). | Protected methods are recorded even when the flag probe fails; the recompile needs only `HarmonyRebuild`. Assumes code that isn't a Harmony replacement never needs recompiling: `sts2.dll` is IL only (no ReadyToRun), tier 0 never inlines, tier 1 needs ~30 calls, and every patch applies before the main menu while the counted methods only run in a run. A late apply (an optional mod loading mid-session) is not followed by a recompile (deferred: ~300 ms of rebuilds mid-run). |
| `CardFlightLimit` | Option `card_flights`, visual: at most N card "souls" per second fly to a pile (burst for small moves); a pile button that got no soul counts its cards itself, once per frame. A reshuffle at 16,800 cards took 5 minutes, frames up to a minute. | Sound and visuals on the local screen only. Up to ~64 cards looks as in the game. The frame a flush was queued in is kept: cards still pending two frames later queue another (Godot drops deferred calls when its message queue is full). A skipped soul returns null from `NCardFlyShuffleVfx.Create`, the game's own test-mode contract (declared nullable; callers use `AddChildSafely`/`?.`): not fingerprinted, the per-version review greps its callers. |
| `GcPacing` | Option `gc_pacing`: in combats with 1,000+ cards, memory is set aside at quiet moments (.NET no-GC region) and quick collections run there instead of during animations. | Memory use only: up to about 1.4 GB more during those combats (older objects only go once the heap grew `FullAfterGrowth`, 1.2 GB, in the combat, plus the 240 MB region; measured 1.2 GB against ~570 MB after a collection; the option text says "up to about 1.4 GB" in the 14 languages). A region start runs a short collection whose length follows the live young objects (`RegionMs`). Combats are counted every 240 frames (a small one keeps its count). `Regions` counts regions that started, refusals are `RegionsRefused`. No game state. |
| `LimitlessHookSkip` | Limitless only, part of `limitless_saves`: a `MultiEnchantment` whose inner enchantments all keep a hook's default is dropped from `FastListeners`' filters for its five forwarded hooks (20,000+ async calls per hook at 10,000 cards). | Own `PatchedByOthers`; off with a changed hook; needs `LimitlessReserialize` applied and arms outside a combat only. Only Limitless's known code may change the inner list: any use of `SetSerializableCards`, the saved list's accessors or either list's field (matched by member handle, in Limitless and in mods referencing it, also loaded later) outside the known writers and users (fingerprinted on 1.2.1) leaves it vanilla; reflection by name can't be scanned. A non-game type on the way declaring an instance `[SavedProperty]` is never dropped. Limit in Step P (only another mod can reach it). |
| `ReplayStateSkip` | Part of `coop_checksum`: above a combat card count, the debugging replay (`replays/latest.mcr`) leaves out its checksum entries, each with its full state (~100 MB per turn at 20,000 cards); actions, choices and hooks stay. Playing such a replay back logs "Replay state diverged … doesn't exist in the replay data" for each one left out. | No game logic reads it; `ChecksumTracker` keeps its own last 20 states. Skips only where the game would add the entry (writer enabled, recording, combat in progress): elsewhere the game's method runs, its own error included. |
| `SyncedCardRelease` | Part of `gc_pacing` (Step U): the cards a co-op sync (at every room entry) replaced in another player's deck leave `RunState._allCards`, where the game kept them for the rest of the run. | Memory only; only `ContainsCard` reads the list, a new reader leaves this vanilla. |
| `DeckCountMemo` | Part of `combat_refresh` (Step T): Squeeze, Perfected Strike and Crescent Spear keep their deck count, updated from pile moves, instead of counting every card at each calculation. | Same count, one answer in 256 compared with the game's; falls back to the game's code when unsure. |
| `CardSortKeys` | Part of `pile_lookup` (game v0.113+): inside a `StableShuffle` of cards (reshuffles, Stone Cracker...), `CardModel.CompareTo` answers with v0.113's logic, the resolved cost and ordered dynamic variable keys computed once per card for the sort. | Same answers, the game's sort: only when every card keeps the game's comparison (interface map included) and every cost listener is a known pure one; where one reads `card.Keywords` (Veilpiercer), every keyword override in that combat must be a fingerprinted adder (Hex, Phantom Blades) no other mod patches, with the keyword code pinned (`CardModel.Keywords`, `GetKeywordsWithSources`, `Hook.ModifyKeywordsInCombat`, the default hook; `CardModel.Pile` too); a comparison off the main thread is the game's; 1 answer in 1,024 compared. Not needed (not applied) on v0.107.1/v0.111.0. See "Game beta v0.113: sorting cards before a shuffle". |
| `ThemeColorSkip` | Part of `combat_refresh`: in the methods refreshing after each action, `AddThemeColorOverride` is skipped when the label already has that color, read from the label itself. | Nothing cached; any change still goes through. |
| `RoomCollect` | Part of `gc_pacing` (v0.10, Step R): the game's `GC.Collect()` at the end of each room's, act's and run's asset loading (`PreloadManager`) runs as a background full collection; with 6 GB+ of memory, .NET's latency mode is `SustainedLowLatency` (full collections in the background unless memory runs short). | Memory only. Off: the game's blocking call and the default mode. The transpiler counts the calls it replaced per method: a method without one keeps the game's code (logged), and with none replaced the patch is skipped before it sets the latency mode. |
| `PooledCardRelease` | Part of `gc_pacing` (v0.10, Step R): card nodes a hand holder took are noted weakly; once a second, their `ModelChanged` handlers targeting a freed node are removed, and a freed card node lets go of its model, preview target and enchantment. Ends a game leak (with BaseLib): every finished combat stayed in memory. | UI only: a freed holder or node can't show or use anything. |
| `ControllerActionSkip` | Part of `relic_input` (v0.10, Step R): in `NInputManager._UnhandledInput`, a controller action missing from the InputMap (left in an old `settings.save`) answers false without asking Godot, which logged an error with a backtrace each time. | Same answer as Godot gives; actions seen once are remembered as present. |
| `FastListeners` kinds | Part of `hook_skip` (v0.10, Step R): filtered walks also leave out listeners outside piles (relics, potions, orbs, powers, monsters, modifiers) whose type keeps the hook's defaults, and a whole kind when no loaded type of it reacts. | Same rule as for pile entries (`tools/hookskip` checks each hook is a no-op for a default listener of any kind); never for filters keeping ExecutionFinished subscribers. `bdperf scan`, `bdperf listeners`. |
| `LimitlessCloneMemo` | Part of `limitless_saves` (v0.10, Step R): Limitless's per-clone rebuild of each saved inner card of a stacked enchantment, kept by content (Step U; per saved card object before) and given again at the next clone. | The result depends only on the saved card (never written after it is made) and BaseLib's saves (stamp); 1 in 256 rebuilt and compared byte for byte; fingerprints of Limitless's `DeepCloneFields` and its lambda (1.1.7 to 1.2.1). The back-off chain also lists the pinned code's callees one level down (`CardModel.AddKeyword/RemoveKeyword`, `CardCmd.RemoveKeyword`, `NCard.FindOnTable`, `CardEnergyCost.UpgradeBy`, `DynamicVar.UpgradeValueBy`, `CardModel.MaxUpgradeLevel`; BaseLib's and Limitless's known patches there allowed by fingerprint); a mod type declaring an instance `[SavedProperty]` is never kept. Limitless's options are read once per outermost `DeepCloneFields` (a per-thread depth), not per inner card. A bounded cache across runs by design (at most 16,384 contents of pure saved data, cleared on a stamp change). `combats3-2k-off/on`. |
| `SavedPropertyLookup` | Part of `coop_checksum` (v0.10, Step R): `SavedProperties.FromInternal` reads each property's `[SavedProperty]` attribute once instead of by reflection at every call (every relic of every player at every co-op checksum). | The attribute's fields are readonly and only read. |
| `RichTextEnvRelease` | Part of `gc_pacing` (Step S): the text effects reading `charFx.Env` (sine, fade-in, fly-in, jitter, thinking dots, and from v0.111 `[scramble]`, `RichTextScramble`: five on v0.107.1, six from v0.111) get that dictionary disposed of when they return, instead of left to its finalizer (one per glyph per frame: the objects that made each ephemeral collection 70-95 ms). | UI only, same values read. Each effect fingerprinted (a changed one stays vanilla; a later-version effect missing is skipped quietly); none disposed while another mod patches one. The optional tracker part (`DisposablesTracker.RegisterDisposable`) failing comes off alone and the effects part stays. `bdperf envrelease on\|off`. |
| `RelicStateReuse` | Part of `coop_checksum` (v0.10, Step R): in `NetFullCombatState.FromRun`, a relic whose serialized content (id, floor, `SavedProperties.From` read again each time) equals its previous object's gives that object again, so BaseLib's postfix no longer adds every relic to its ConditionalWeakTable at every action (that table grows with every snapshot the replay keeps). | Content compared in full at every snapshot, nothing tracked; only while no BaseLib relic save is registered (its data then writes nothing), no saved cards, no other mod's patch on `ToSerializable` / `SerializableRelic.Serialize` / `SavedProperties.From`, nor (fourth 2026-11-15 pass) on `SavedProperties.Serialize` / `NetFullCombatState.RelicState.Serialize`, which write a reused object again (Unlimited's list-width transpiler allowed by fingerprint; resolved on its own, `UnlimitedCompat`, so SnapshotCardReuse's allowance no longer depends on this patch applying). Fingerprints (v0.107.1 to v0.113.x): `ToSerializable` `91A31F3A5C8BCB32`, `Serialize` `FEE0E78D320AB4F9`, BaseLib's relic postfix `A0902464D285F2CB`, `WriteExtended` and `ExtendedSaveHandlers.Write` (BaseLib 3.4.7 to 3.4.8); the Scalar part also `FromInternal` (`F73EEC2208D6E517`, the betas' equivalent `B8EF45DA081344A5`), `ShouldSerialize`, `GetTypeDefaultValue` and BaseLib's `SavedSpireFieldPatch.PostfixFromInternal`/`GetFieldsForModel`; Unlimited's transpiler `908CDF014E4F04CF` and `IsVanillaListBitsConstant` `CB9DCCF74112728F` (the same in 1.0.3 and 2.0.0). Scalar getters compiled once per getter method (not per relic type); a plan that can't be built leaves that type to the game. A skipped part logs "left vanilla" (`ScalarNotApplied`; bdopt lists it once ModEntry reads it). `relics-verify-0..3`: every relic, every snapshot against the game's own. |
| `CoopModeTag` | Co-op players must all have the mod on, or all off: with the whole mod off (master switch off, or off when the game started), a postfix on `ModManager.GetGameplayRelevantModNameList` (a new list per call on the four versions) appends `-off` to this mod's `id-version` entry. The game's join check compares the host's list with the client's and refuses a difference ("mod mismatch", naming both lists), so an on and an off player can't play together; two off players can, and anyone can host. | Applied first, and even with the mod off at start (the only patch then). No game state: the list is only the join check's (v0.107.1: the host's `InitialGameInfoMessage` per join and the client's check; v0.111+: `PeerVersionInfo.LocalDefault()`, taken when the host or join screen creates its net service, so a switch turned after that isn't seen until the next host or join). Entry not found (a format change): the game's list, logged once. Counter `Tagged` (test builds). |

All patches catch their own exceptions and fall back to vanilla.

Changes to existing patches (2026-10-30, Step O):

- `FastListeners`: affliction changes of a card in a pile bump `AfflictionVersion` instead of `PileVersion` when the old and new affliction types are both known (types are recorded the first time they are seen, by the setter or a snapshot rebuild; a change involving a new type bumps `PileVersion` as before). Combat snapshots depend on both versions (an affliction change rebuilds them, no catch-up across one); a filter's segment survives affliction changes when the filter keeps no subscriber and reacts to none of the known affliction types (`AfflictionFree`: the known set only grows, so free now means free of every change since). Hex no longer rebuilds `ShouldAfflict`'s entries for each card. `HookFilter`'s walked lists are keyed on `EntriesVersion` (both versions). `bdperf segments verify all` (`party5-verify`): 0 mismatch over ~107,000 cached walks.
- `PileIndex`: the owner's piles are read without `Player.Piles`' `Concat` iterator (two allocations per `CardModel.Pile` call) while that getter has its v0.107.1/v0.111 code (`_runPiles` field, combat `AllPiles`); otherwise through the getter as before.
- `HookFilter`: `ForCombat`/`ForRun` look the walked list up before creating the walk lambdas (two closures per call, even on cache hits: ~70 MB per 13 s of co-op plays at 4,100 cards).
- `ListenerCache`: materialized listener lists are sized from the previous count (thousands of entries grown by doubling allocated twice as much, the last arrays on the large object heap).
- `Settings`: parts of an option (`LimitlessRecalcSkip`, `SnapshotCardReuse`, `TextPoolReuse`) turn themselves off alone after an error (`Part`), and don't make their option unavailable when not applied.

### Back-offs visible before they happen (2026-11-15)

A player with a big mod pack (RitsuLib, Zone the Spire, Spire Synergies, Ascended Expansion, Master of Mankind, CVC...) reported the mod "isn't working": nothing broke, but the back-offs turned most combat optimizations off, unseen. They were recorded only when each optimization first checked its methods (the first combat), some weren't recorded at all (another mod's types or data), and the menu still showed every box ticked.

- **Registry** (`PatchSafety.Watch`, `WatchAll`, `WatchPairs`, `Patches/PatchSafetyWatch.cs`): each patch registers, where its Apply already resolved them, the methods its runtime `IsPatchedByOthers` checks pass, with the same allowed lists (a lazy list where the patch builds it once mods are loaded, e.g. HookSkip's BaseLib patches, CardDbScan's Limitless ledger, CardFlightLimit's SpeedX postfix), under the name the runtime check records (its file name). Registering only stores; it never throws (it runs inside Apply, where a throw would take the patch away). Methods known only later are sources asked at each preflight: the walk code (ListenerCache), HookSkip's helper profiles and the combat hook walk (once a hook's call to it is redirected), BaseLib's card modifier subscribers, the clone chain (LimitlessCloneMemo), pools (PoolSignalScan), and what depends on who is in a combat: the listener types met so far (CardSortKeys' cost hooks, SnapshotCardReuse's pure overrides, DeckCountMemo's per-type getters, LimitlessCloneMemo's overrides on the way). Not registered: CardSortKeys' keyword reads (only checked in a combat where a cost hook reads keywords).
- **Preflight** (`PatchSafety.Preflight`): checks every registered entry with the runtime checks' own code (`CheckPatched`, the two `IsPatchedByOthers` overloads forward to it, `AggressiveInlining`: same hot-path cost), so a back-off is recorded under the right patch; display only: the runtime checks keep deciding, sharing the same 2 s cache (same computation, same answer). At startup `PreflightOverFrames` starts after `Patches applied` is logged (outside its timing), waits for the main menu, then runs within 3 ms per frame (a run starting meanwhile gets the rest at once) and logs `Preflight: N methods and notes checked in X ms over Y frames`. The settings page (`SetupConfigUI`, before its texts) and `bdopt` run it again synchronously (cached answers reused). Nothing before every patch applied (allowed lists are built once mods loaded) or when the mod started off.
- **Notes** (`PatchSafety.Note`/`ClearNote`, checks registered with `WatchNote`, run by the preflight only): silent back-offs, with the mods responsible. BaseLib card modifier types (FastListeners' subscriber kept, ListenerCache's cross-scope reuse, LimitlessReserialize's card modifier save; the mods defining them, `BaseLibTypes.DefiningMods`), BaseLib type line modifiers (TypePlaqueSkip), SavedSpireFields on cards or enchantments (LimitlessReserialize's plans, SnapshotCardReuse's memo, LimitlessRecalcSkip's plain types; the mod is the code behind the field's default value, else its target type's mod), other mods' BaseLib card/enchantment saves (no memo: SnapshotCardReuse, LimitlessReserialize) and relic saves (RelicStateReuse off; the mod is each save's getter code), and other mods' types met in combats that leave a sort (CardSortKeys: own sort order, cost hooks not known to only read) or the pure reads (SnapshotCardReuse) to the game.
- **Display**: `Settings.ConflictingMods`/`Conflict` include the notes' mods ("other mods" when a note names none, key `other_mods`); the settings page shows a line under the co-op note when anything is recorded (`Settings.ConflictSummary`, key `conflict_summary`: each mod with its count of back-offs and notes); `bdopt why` (`Settings.WhyReport`) lists them; `bdperf conflicts` prints the same with the preflight counters (Debug).
- Not proved in a game yet: see the roadmap's checks for it.

### Foreign patches judged by their shape (2026-11-15)

**Not released: audited (2026-11-16, the rules tightened below, "Audit of the shape rules"), still to be proven in game** (synthetic shapes A/B, `coop-foreign` with `parcoop -OnOff`, `BDTEST_EXTRA_MODS` with RitsuLib, Zone the Spire, SynergyMod and CardAscension on v0.107.1 and v0.113.1, solo and co-op).

The same player's mod pack (previous section) kept most combat optimizations vanilla through back-offs from patches that can't change what this mod relies on. No per-mod list and no fingerprint of another mod's code (each of their updates would need checking again): the rules are generic, decided from what Harmony says about each foreign patch (its kind; its method's return type and parameters) and, for type gates only, a strict IL shape. A patch matching no rule keeps today's back-off.

- **`PatchTolerance.SurroundingOnly`** (`Patches/PatchSafetyTolerance.cs`, `PatchSafety.IsPatchedByOthers(method, [allowed,] tolerance)`): only at call sites where the original always runs and this mod's code around it keeps its effect identical (no prefix of this mod skips it: HookSkip's transpiled `Hook` methods, the pile methods). Let stand: a **postfix** whose parameters are all by value except `__result` (the caller gets what it leaves, as without this mod) and `__state` (its own prefix's), not taking `__args` (Harmony's `RestoreArgumentArray` writes the array back into the argument slots) nor `__runOriginal` (it differs wherever a prefix of this mod may skip the original); a by-reference argument or instance is the replacement's own argument slot (`ldarga`), which this mod's finalizers read after every postfix (PileIndex, FastListeners, CardDbScan on `AddInternal`). A **prefix returning void** with the same by-value parameters (only `__state` by reference: SynergyMod's `AddInternal` prefix), not taking `__args`, and sorted by Harmony (`PatchProcessor.GetSortedPatchMethods`: priority, before/after, registration) before **every** prefix of this mod on the method, each of its copies (`PatchInfo.Add` doesn't deduplicate: the last copy decides), except this mod's `[OrderFreePrefix]` watches (PileIndex's and CardDbScan's `WatchAdd`/`WatchRemove`: the pile count they take is only read when the tracking prefix didn't run). Before every prefix, not only the skipping ones: one sorted after a tracking prefix runs between this mod's bookkeeping and the list change (a walk it makes cached at the version the change is read at, a nested add recorded out of the pile's order); one after a skipping prefix runs after the mirror's computation, or not at all (Harmony skips a prefix that "affects the original", `MethodCreatorTools.AffectsOriginal`, once an earlier one returned false). The tracking prefixes on `AddInternal`/`RemoveInternal` are therefore late (`Priority.Last + 1` for FastListeners' bump and CardDbScan's `RecordAdd`, `Priority.Last` for PileIndex's update, their order among themselves unchanged): another mod's prefix at the default priority runs before them. Not let stand anywhere: bool prefixes, by-ref prefixes, transpilers, finalizers, inner prefixes and postfixes, factory patch methods (returning the patch to use: Harmony runs them, and its sorter would too), patches with a `HarmonyArgument` on the method, a parameter or the class (Harmony binds the real name, `InjectedParameter.CalculateRealName`: a harmless-looking parameter can be `__args`, `__state` or an argument), a patch whose method can't be found or classified.
- **`PatchTolerance.PrefixesOnly`**: for a call site where a prefix of this mod answers for the original and skips it (HookFilter's mirrored hooks and cost color helpers): only the void prefixes SurroundingOnly lets stand. No postfix: behind a skipping prefix Harmony still runs every postfix, where the game would not always (a listener exception the mirror passes on, `PassOn`/`RethrowFinalizer`: the game runs no postfix after a throwing original; a postfix there could also call another mirrored hook whose finalizer throws the outer exception and a `try` in the postfix swallow it), and a postfix reading `__runOriginal` sees false there.
- **`PatchTolerance.TypeGated`** (`PatchSafety.IsPatchedByOthers(method, out Type[] gates)`): only for an `AbstractModel` default whose calls this mod skips for listener types keeping it. A postfix there would miss the skipped calls, so only a prefix or postfix that provably does nothing unless `__instance` is of some types stands, and those types are reported. `PatchSafety.TypeGates` (analysed once per patch method, `MethodFingerprint.Instructions` decodes the IL): from the first instruction, nops aside, only tests `ldarg __instance; isinst T; [stloc n; ldloc n;] brfalse|brtrue L` along the path a non-matching instance takes (isinst gives null: brfalse taken, brtrue falls through), unconditional branches, then the no-op exit: `ret` for a void patch, `ldc.i4.1; ret` for a bool prefix. Nothing else on that path: no call, no field or static write, no other argument read; or-ed tests (`is A || is B`, a `switch` on the type) chain; a postfix returning a value never matches; T a class or interface, not generic. Also refused: a patch whose own method is patched by anyone (the code that runs is its replacement, not the IL read; asked at each recheck, not cached), one whose class has a static constructor (`TypeInitializer`: its first call would run it at another time, or never; none of SynergyMod's gated classes has one), one renaming its parameters (`__instance` found by its declared name only once no `HarmonyArgument` can rebind it). A detour made without Harmony isn't seen. Checked offline on Release-compiled copies of the report's shapes (`if (__instance is Stomp stomp && ...)`, `if (!(__instance is Stomp stomp) || ...) return true;`, `is A || is B`, a type switch, an early return) and near misses (a static write or a call before the test, another argument tested, the negated test doing work, a bool prefix returning false, `GetType() ==`, an empty patch): all as intended.
- Every answer shares PatchSafety's 2 s cache; a check with a tolerance first reads the plain check's answer, so with no other mod's patch the cost is the plain check's. With one let stand, a cached answer costs the tolerant dictionary lookup and, while the same call site asks, no logging lookup (`TolerantAnswer.LoggedFor`). The prefix order is Harmony's sorter run once per change of a method's prefix list (`Precedence`, keyed by owners, indexes, priorities, before/after and methods), with each patch's method set from `PatchMethodOf`'s cache first (the sorter otherwise resolves each one through `AccessTools.GetMethodByModuleAndToken`, a search of every loaded module), never with a factory in the list (the sorter would run it). A back-off from a tolerant check is recorded and logged "(postfixes and void prefixes not enough)" / "(void prefixes not enough)" / "(patches gated on the listener's type not enough)", keyed by tolerance too (a patch checking one method strictly for one part and tolerantly for another doesn't flip its record at each call); patches let stand are logged once per patch, method and mods ("... (postfixes and void prefixes: run around this mod's code as without it): kept", gated ones with "X treated as overriding it"). Every changed check registers its tolerance with `PatchSafety.Watch` (same arguments), so the preflight shows the same answers. Test builds count `PatchSafety.ToleratedChecks`, `GateAnalyses`, `GatedMethods`, and per runtime call site (the preflight passes a patch name and isn't counted) the checks kept and backed off (`PatchSafety.RuntimeReport`), and HookSkip's walks filtered with gates per hook (`HookSkip.GatedWalkReport`); `bdperf conflicts` lists what was let stand (`PatchSafety.ToleratedReport`).

Where applied, and why it's exact there:

- **HookSkip, the `Hook` method itself** (SurroundingOnly, allowed BaseLib patches kept): the transpiled body calls the same listeners with the same effects, only no-op calls left out, so a postfix (RitsuLib's ~53 lifecycle postfixes wrapping the returned task, `ref Task __result` and arguments by value; Zone the Spire's, CardAscension's) or an earlier void prefix sees the state it sees without this mod. On a hook that also carries a HookFilter mirror (a bool prefix of this mod), a void prefix must precede it here too. **Not** on an async hook's state machine (`MoveNext`): a patch there can read the machine's fields, the walk's enumerator among them, which the redirect makes another object. Left as is: a postfix of an async hook runs once the hook first awaits, the walk suspended, and a subscription it made then to a later dropped listener's `ExecutionFinished` isn't signalled (`Entry.Subscribed`'s accepted gap, which a listener's own code already had; no mod read subscribes).
- **HookSkip, the `AbstractModel` defaults** (TypeGated): `FilterFor` gives a filter in which types assignable to a gate also react (`PileFilter.WithGates`: a new filter with its own caches, segments and kind verdicts, kept while the gates stay the same; stacked Limitless enchantments are never dropped there, their inner calls reach the patched default and LimitlessHookSkip's inner check doesn't know the gates). Gate types keep going through the game's call, the patch running as without this mod; every other type's call would have done nothing (the patch's no-op path, then the default). Several gated defaults of one hook: their gates combined once per set of PatchSafety answers (`Entry.LastCombined`, compared by reference: no allocation per walk, and the same array for `WithGates`).
- **HookFilter, the mirrored hooks and the two cost color helpers** (PrefixesOnly): the mirror's prefix answers for the original and skips it, so only void prefixes Harmony runs before it stand (as before the original without this mod; one at the same priority registered later runs after the mirror and backs off, `foreign-controls`); every postfix backs off (PrefixesOnly above). `_passOn` isn't cleared at a mirror's entry: with no other mod's postfix or finalizer there, nothing runs another mirrored hook between `PassOn` and `RethrowFinalizer`, and clearing it would let a nested mirror erase an outer pending exception. A caller using these lists to skip the hook call altogether keeps the strict check on the hook (`HookFilter.HookMethodOf`): SnapshotCardReuse's fast reads (local keywords and cost, reused pile states) check `Hook.ModifyKeywordsInCombat` and `Hook.ModifyEnergyCostInCombat` with no tolerance, since a skipped call skips even a void prefix.
- **PileIndex, `CardPile.AddInternal`/`RemoveInternal`** (the self-check that also makes `PlacesIn` unsure, SurroundingOnly): a tolerated patch can't skip the original or change its arguments (nor the argument slots the finalizer reads afterwards: postfixes by value), so `BeforeAdd`/`BeforeRemove` (Priority.Last, void) always run and the finalizer judges throws from the list (`Judge`); a card a postfix moves goes through these methods again and is counted there. The in-flight check (`_moveOthers`, a card mid-move gets the game's scan) still sees any patch, without recording a back-off (`PatchSafety.HasOtherPatches`): it isn't one, the index keeps answering. Clear, the moves and the shuffle keep no tolerance. Under the self-check (a changed list method), a postfix's nested add to the same pile makes the count check fail: the index faults, the game's scan from then on.
- **FastListeners' tracked code, `AddInternal`/`RemoveInternal`** (SurroundingOnly): they were listed because an earlier prefix returning false makes Harmony skip the tracking prefix (it takes the card); a tolerated patch can't. A tolerated void prefix runs before `BeforeAdd`/`BeforeRemove` (now `Priority.Last + 1`): a walk it makes is cached at the version before the bump, never at the bumped version with the pile not yet changed. A card moved by a postfix bumps `PileVersion` twice more inside the outer call, whose finalizer then sees `PileVersion != From + 2` and records no move: the segments are rebuilt. The getters (`Cards`, `Enchantment`, `Affliction`) and the other writers keep no tolerance.
- **CardDbScan, `AddInternal`** (SurroundingOnly, both at combat start and per walk): `RecordAdd` (a prefix taking the card) always runs; a card a patch puts in a pile goes through `AddInternal` again and is recorded, its own `ContentsChanged` walks it; a tolerated void prefix runs before `RecordAdd` (now `Priority.Last + 1`), so a card it adds to the same pile is recorded first, in the pile's order; the 1-in-64 whole-pile check stays. The walk and `IdCardIfNecessary` keep no tolerance (the walk is skipped, a patch there or on the calls it makes would miss them).
- **CardSortKeys, the cost hooks' defaults** (TypeGated): a sort runs the cost hook once per card instead of per comparison; a gated patch stands while no listener of the combat is of a gate type (then each of its calls does nothing, however many); a gate-type listener refuses that sort ("... gated on it").

Not applied, and why (each call site says so in a comment): HookSkip's choice-context helpers and their state machines (a dropped listener skips its calls to them), BaseLib's hand size/heal/downgrade methods (a void prefix after HookSkip's would take the pending filter for its own walk), the walk code (`IterateHookListeners`, its iterators, `Contains`, ModHelper's subscriber walks, `Hook.IterateCombatHookListeners` and the getters it reads: a postfix changes what the mirrored or replayed walk yields), every getter this mod answers from its own state or reads around (`CardModel.Pile`, `Player.Piles`, `CardPile.Cards`, `Enchantment`, `Affliction`, keywords, `GetWithModifiers`, DeckCountMemo's getters), the serializers SnapshotCardReuse, RelicStateReuse and LimitlessReserialize reuse output of, every method whose call this mod skips or merges (HookFilter's defaults, SnapshotCardReuse's pure overrides, DeckCountMemo, CardFlightLimit, HoverTipReuse, IdleButtonInput, LimitlessCloneMemo, LimitlessRecalcSkip, LimitlessHookSkip, ReplayStateSkip, RelicFlashMerge, TypePlaqueSkip, PileCounterBatch, RichTextEnvRelease, ExtraScopes, PoolSignalScan, EnchantmentTabReuse), PileIndex's `RunState.ContainsCard` and Clear/moves. HookFilter's defaults keep no tolerance at all, gates included: its per-type override masks are cached for the session and its lists per scope, SnapshotCardReuse's pile trust reads those lists (a gate-type listener would be trusted as pure while the gated patch runs at each cost read), so gates there need both keyed by the gate set first. SnapshotCardReuse has no default check of its own (its pure listeners come from HookFilter), and no tolerance on the keyword and cost hooks it skips. Every SurroundingOnly site relies on "no code but the game's writers changes `CardPile._cards`" (`RequireFieldWriters`, game code only): a tolerated postfix writing that list directly (reflection, a field reference) isn't told apart from its shape; a mod doing it from any other code (a `ContentsChanged` handler) was never seen either. PileIndex's count check stays off with tolerated patches (on, a nested add through `AddInternal` would fault it); CardDbScan's sampled whole-pile check stays.

Real cases. SynergyMod from its shipped DLL (`ilspycmd -il`; the first count came from the decompiled C#, which hides a copy of an argument into a local before the type test): 88 patches on `AbstractModel` defaults, **75 gated** (the auditor's replica of `AnalyseGates` over the IL), 13 not: 5 postfixes on `ModifyCardPlayCount`, 2 patches on `BeforeCardPlayed` wrapped in `try` or its `SynergyPatchSafety.Run`, and 6 starting `ldarg.k; stloc.0` before `ldarg.0; isinst` (StompZeroCostPlayedPatch and RebootPlayableCheckPatch on `AfterCardPlayed`, StompReturnNextTurnPatch on `AfterPlayerTurnStart`, HappyFlowerPotionAdvancePatch on `AfterPotionUsed`, RebootExtraTurnConsumedPatch on `AfterTakingExtraTurn`, RebootExtraTurnRequestPatch on `ShouldTakeExtraTurn`): those hooks stay vanilla, `AfterCardPlayed` among them (accepting such a pure copy prologue would recover them; not done, the rule kept as strict as checked). None of its gated classes has a static constructor or a `HarmonyArgument`. Its `CardPile.AddInternal` void prefix (default priority, `ref __state`, card by value) now precedes this mod's tracking prefixes and its postfixes (by value) and `RemoveInternal` postfix are let stand; its 8 `Hook` patches (postfixes, void prefixes taking `CardPlay`/`CardModel` by value) are let stand by HookSkip except a default-priority void prefix on a hook carrying a HookFilter mirror. CardAscension patches no `AbstractModel` default (its ShreddedSoul gates test a `card` argument): 0 type-gated matches; its 4 `Hook` postfixes (`ModifyKeywordsInCombat`, `ModifyCardPlayResultLocation`, `ModifyPowerAmountGiven`, `BeforeCombatStart`) are let stand by HookSkip, while HookFilter's keyword mirror and SnapshotCardReuse's keyword reads now back off from the first; its bool prefixes elsewhere still back off. Zone the Spire's 6 `Hook` postfixes and RitsuLib's lifecycle postfixes (`ref Task __result`, arguments by value): let stand by HookSkip; HookFilter backs off from any postfix on a mirrored hook (Zone's `ShouldAllowHitting`). **Expected** for that player (not measured): the pile index, the cached filtered walks and the card id walks back on with SynergyMod; most hooks filtered again, but not `ModifyCardPlayCount`, `BeforeCardPlayed`, `AfterCardPlayed`, `AfterPlayerTurnStart`, `AfterPotionUsed`, `AfterTakingExtraTurn`, `ShouldTakeExtraTurn` and those another of their patches keeps vanilla; HookFilter's damage and energy cost mirrors stay vanilla inside refresh scopes (SynergyMod patches their defaults), its keyword and hitting mirrors too (CardAscension's, Zone's postfixes); CardSortKeys still the game's (CardAscension's `GetWithModifiers` prefix).

Less for CrossVersionCompat to rewrite: `tools/compat` on the Release DLL listed, as BDO's only references to game members missing on v0.113.0/v0.113.1, FastListeners' two `[PatchPart("scaling")]` tails (`CombatState`/`RunState.MultiplayerScalingModel`). They now read the property through compiled getters made only on v0.107.1/v0.111.0 (`Getter<>`, as v0.113's run models and dominion), the same object (an auto-property; the combat tail reads it once instead of twice); the walk fingerprints choosing the tail are unchanged. `tools/compat`: 0 problem and no unresolved part on v0.107.1, v0.111.0, v0.113.0 and v0.113.1.

Tests (`Bench/ForeignPatches.cs`; script `foreignpatch`, console `bdperf foreign`; Harmony ids `bdtest.foreign.<shape>`; each install logs `FOREIGN installed` and `PatchSafety.Describe`'s verdict on every other mod's patch of the method, `FOREIGN classify ...: SurroundingOnly tolerated|backs off (why); PrefixesOnly ...; TypeGated ...`; `foreignpatch report` adds, Debug only, `FOREIGN runtime <Patch> <Type.Method> (<tolerance>) kept N backed off M` from the runtime checks alone and `FOREIGN gated walks Hook.X N`):

- Tolerated: `noop-postfix`, `noop-prefix [priority N]`, `count-postfix`, `count-prefix` (calls counted per target), `nested-postfix` (on `CardPile:AddInternal`: a card added to a discard pile takes the draw pile's last card along through `RemoveInternal` + `AddInternal`, the game's own methods), with effects the checksums see: `keyword-prefix squeeze` (void, priority 800, on `Hook:ModifyKeywordsInCombat`: Squeeze gets Retain; HookFilter's mirror and HookSkip let it stand, SnapshotCardReuse's local keyword reads must back off or the checksums differ), `cost-postfix squeeze` (`ref __result` on `Hook:ModifyEnergyCostInCombat`: Squeeze costs 1 more; HookSkip lets it stand, HookFilter and SnapshotCardReuse back off), `probe-prefix`/`probe-postfix` (the added card's cost before and after each `CardPile:AddInternal`, at the default priority: a hook walk over the cached piles while a card moves, summed as STATE lines), `state-prefix` (SynergyMod's `AddInternal` shape: a void prefix taking `ref __state`, its postfix summing the pile's growth), `count-prefix` also on the async `Hook:BeforeCardPlayed` (RitsuLib's `Before*` shape), `gated-postfix squeeze|relic`, `gated-prefix squeeze`, `gated-or-postfix squeeze-relic` (Squeeze overrides no hook; gated calls counted per target; two gated defaults of `AfterCardPlayed`, three of `AfterPlayerTurnStart`: combined gates). The gated shapes live in a class without a static constructor (`ForeignPatches.Gates`). `foreignpatch report <label>` prints the counts as STATE lines (compare.py compares them), then the lines above; `foreignpatch require tolerated` fails unless a check let one stand.
