- Must back off: `bool-prefix` (returns true), `ref-prefix` (`ref __result`, or `ref __runOriginal` on a void method), `identity-transpiler`, `noop-prefix` at the default priority on a HookFilter mirror (sorted after it) or at priority 0 on `RemoveInternal` (after the tracking prefixes), `noop-postfix` on a HookFilter mirror (HookFilter backs off, HookSkip keeps it), `runoriginal-postfix`, `byref-postfix` (`ref CardModel card`), `argsrename-prefix` (`[HarmonyArgument("__args")]`), `factory-postfix`, `dup-prefix` (one method registered at priority 800 and again at the default: the later copy decides), near misses of a type gate: `nearmiss-postfix squeeze` (a static write before the test; also in the A/B, every call counted as a STATE line, so a near miss taken as gated would change a count), `renamed-gated-postfix squeeze` (`[HarmonyArgument("card")] __instance`), `gated-patched-postfix squeeze` (its own method patched), `cctor-gated-postfix squeeze` (a static constructor); `inner-postfix` (classified only: Harmony 2.4.2 can register one, `PatchProcessor.AddInnerPostfix`, but nothing gives it its inner method, so Harmony can't build it; a synthetic `HarmonyLib.Patches` goes through `PatchSafety.Describe(method, patches)`, the listing of every kind's list a real method gets). `foreignpatch classify <Type:Method>`, `foreignpatch remove`.
- Scenarios: `foreign-shapes-2k-on`/`-off` (ab-2k with the tolerated shapes and the near miss installed after the imports: CHECKSUM and STATE lines, the foreign counts and sums included, must match line for line; the on side expects the "kept" and back-off lines, positive counts for every gated shape, the probes and the near miss, `FOREIGN runtime` lines for each site (kept, or backed off where the rule says) and `FOREIGN gated walks` on the gated hooks, `effectcheck` HookSkip, PileIndex, FastListeners.Cache and HookFilter grew, `foreignpatch require tolerated`; the off side alone runs `bdperf checks off`: no counting shape on the cost color helpers, which the Debug re-check calls again), `foreign-controls` (the shapes that must back off, each on its own hook so a default's runtime check isn't cut short by a control on its hook: classification lines and `FOREIGN runtime ... kept 0 backed off N` lines the preflight can't produce), `coop-foreign-host`/`-client` (coop with tolerated shapes on both peers, `parcoop -OnOff`: the mod-off twins keep the `foreignpatch` lines, only `foreignpatch require` goes). `fakeconflict <Type:Method> [boolprefix]`: `conflict-check` puts a bool prefix on the cost color helper (a postfix there backs off too now).


#### Audit of the shape rules (2026-11-16)

An adversarial audit (four parts: Harmony semantics, call sites, gates, tests; 31 findings, each confirmed by a verifier) of 08b0c48. The owner's stance: no per-mod list, generic rules; where equivalence with the game running those mods without this mod isn't provable at a call site, tighten the rule there rather than add machinery. What changed:

- **HookFilter's mirrors and cost color helpers: no postfix** (`PrefixesOnly`). Behind a skipping prefix Harmony runs every postfix, also on the listener-exception path where the game runs none (and a postfix calling another mirrored hook could get the outer exception thrown at it and swallow it), and hands them `__runOriginal` false. Only void prefixes sorted before every prefix of this mod stand there.
- **SnapshotCardReuse keeps the strict meaning**: its fast reads skip `Hook.ModifyKeywordsInCombat`/`ModifyEnergyCostInCombat` altogether, so it checks them with no tolerance itself (it had inherited HookFilter's: a keyword or cost postfix would have been missing from the co-op checksum snapshot, CardAscension's shape).
- **Prefix order against every prefix of this mod** on the method, not only the skipping ones (a void prefix after FastListeners' bump cached the pile before the change at the version of the change), every copy of a patch registered twice, `[OrderFreePrefix]` watches aside; the tracking prefixes on `AddInternal`/`RemoveInternal` made late so other mods' default-priority prefixes still precede them. The order is computed once per change of the prefix list, without Harmony's module search, never running a factory.
- **Postfix parameters** (SurroundingOnly): by value only, `__result` and `__state` aside; no `__args`, no `__runOriginal` (the finalizers read the argument slots after the postfixes).
- **Real names**: any `HarmonyArgument` on the patch method, a parameter or its class backs off (all tolerances, the gates' `__instance` included).
- **Gates**: refused when the patch method is itself patched (checked at each recheck), when its class has a static constructor, when it renames parameters; SynergyMod's count corrected from the shipped DLL (75 gated, 6 more hooks vanilla). Combined gates cached per set of answers (no allocation per walk).
- **Factory patch methods** back off everywhere.
- **Tests** can now fail when a rule is wrong: shapes with effects the checksums see on each tolerated site, a near miss counted in the A/B, controls each on its own hook with runtime-only `FOREIGN runtime` lines, `inner-postfix` through PatchSafety's own listing, a co-op pair whose mod-off twins keep the foreign patches.

What remains, accepted and documented at the call sites: a postfix of an async `Hook` method subscribing to a later dropped listener's `ExecutionFinished` during the suspended walk (`Entry.Subscribed`'s gap, which listeners' own code already had); a tolerated patch writing `CardPile._cards` directly (no shape rule sees it; the same as any other mod code doing it); a detour made without Harmony on a gated patch method; SynergyMod's six `ldarg; stloc` prologue gates left vanilla. Not tested: a throwing listener under a mirrored hook with a foreign patch (no postfix stands there any more; a void prefix runs before the mirror as before the original), interface and enchantment gates (Limitless's `_noMultiDrop` path), the cost color helpers' runtime lines (the helpers run for the UI). None of the scenarios has been run yet.
## Console commands

Public (`src/BigDeckOptimization/Commands`, in every build):

- `bdopt [on|off]` — every optimization on/off at runtime (vanilla when off), or the state of each one (incl. patches skipped or self-disabled). Local. Each option gives the same results on or off, so peers may differ, with one exception: the whole mod off during a co-op run also turns off the fast combat start the other players keep, so this player starts each combat far later and the game can drop the actions reaching a player still starting a combat (desync or a stuck turn, "Co-op with a player without the mod"). There `bdopt off` prints a warning and needs `bdopt off yes`, the menu's master switch can't be unticked, and `bdopt` warns while the mod is off in a co-op run. "Co-op run" is `RunManager.IsInProgress` and not singleplayer: a co-op lobby before the run starts isn't detected (the game has no static access to it), so `bdopt off` there doesn't warn. Joining is guarded instead: with the whole mod off, `CoopModeTag` makes the game refuse a player whose on/off state differs from the host's.
- `bdopt <option> on|off` — one optimization: any option key, `bdopt` with no argument lists them (20: `instant_combats`, `fast_combat_start`, `coop_checksum`, `combat_refresh`, `hook_filter`, `relic_input`, `relic_flashes`, `card_flights`, `gc_pacing`, `grid_sort`, `grid_pool`, `pile_lookup`, `card_ids`, `pile_counters`, `listeners`, `hook_skip`, `hover_tips`, `limitless_tabs`, `limitless_saves`, `card_type_line`); `bdopt fast_combat_start_relics <0-100>` sets the fast combat start's relic threshold (BaseLib slider). Saved in BaseLib's mod configuration. Never re-enables a patch that turned itself off after an error.
- `bdopt why` — every part of an optimization another mod keeps on the normal game, grouped by option: `Patch: Type.Method <- Mod1, Mod2` for a back-off from a method another mod patches, a sentence for a note (another mod's types or saved data), then a count per mod. `bdopt` ends with a line pointing to it whenever anything is recorded. Both check first (`Settings.RefreshConflicts`, answers younger than 2 s reused).
- `bdopt` is not a debug-only command (`DebugOnly => false`): since game v0.113 a debug command marks the run as having used one in its save (`ExtraFields.MarkDebugToolUsed`), even to show the state.

## Test tooling (`src/BigDeckOptimization/Bench`, Debug builds only: excluded from Release by the csproj, `BENCH` symbol)

- `bigdeck <count> [card-id] [seed]` — fill the deck up to count (one card, or random from the character pool). Networked + seeded (seed 0 default): usable in co-op if **every** player has the mod.
- `bigdeck relics <count> [relic-id] [seed]` — add relics (character + shared pools), no obtain effects. Networked.
- `bigdeck import <file.run|shared-name> [all|i|i,j] [cards|relics|all] [hash]` — exact decks (upgrades, enchantments) and relics from a run history file, added like a save load (no obtain/card-added hooks). Players can be merged. In co-op: only bare names from the shared folder `user://bigdeck` (`%APPDATA%\SlayTheSpire2\bigdeck`), and the SHA-256 prefix from `bigdeck hash <name>` is required: the peers that import provably read the same bytes, but a peer whose file is missing, unreadable (a locked file is a refusal, not an exception in the action queue) or different refuses with an error while the others import, and the run diverges: run `bigdeck hash` on every machine first. Barrier marks (`bigdeck mark`) are cleared at the first mark made in another run.
- `bigdeck tank [hp]` — sets every living enemy's max and current HP (default 1,000,000) for long perf-test combats. Networked.
- `bigdeck hash <shared-name>` — prints the SHA-256 prefix of a shared run file (each peer prints its own).
- Caps: deck ≤ 5000, ≤ 1000 relics per command (any peer can issue networked commands).
- `bdperf checksum [n]` — checksum cost, rows vanilla (the whole mod off), others, cache, cache+filter, +card reuse; checks identical checksums. Every bench forcing a part on puts its switch back only if the part didn't fault meanwhile, refuses a part that faulted earlier in the session (`faulted earlier in the session ... nothing to compare`), and restores the effect counters afterwards (`Bench/EffectCounters`: the `EFFECT` lines count only the game's own calls).
- `bdperf refresh [n]` — vanilla vs cached post-action refresh (enchantments + hand), checks the game state is identical afterwards; `not applied` when ListenerCache isn't, `not compared` when nothing of this mod served on the cached side.
- `bdperf verify [off|sample|all]` — live verification mode and counters (snapshots, verified, mismatches), patch on/off state. `all` costs a vanilla snapshot per action: singleplayer debugging only.
- `bdperf grid [cards]` — deck view sort orders (vanilla vs cached, identical orders), enchantment tab rendering on cards spread over the deck (vanilla vs reuse, identical tab state), and freeing grid holders and their card nodes to the pool (vanilla vs scan, identical remaining connections, a row each; everything freed before the bench returns). Bench timings of rendering/freeing are far below in-game ones (hidden container, no GC pressure): use them for identity, traces for gains. UI only, usable anywhere in a run.
- `bdperf combatstart` — wall-clock breakdown of the last combat start (phases, GC pauses, longest frames). `checksum` and `refresh` refuse to run in real co-op.
- `bdperf opt on|off` — all optimizations on/off at runtime for an A/B comparison in the same session; never saved (BaseLib saves the player's choice, `Settings.MasterChoice`, at a settings change and on quit; the menu box keeps showing it). In a co-op run `bdperf opt off` warns like `bdopt off` and needs `bdperf opt off yes`.
- `bdperf costwrites` — makes the first hand card free this turn, on this peer only (a state change): singleplayer only, refused in co-op like `checksum` and `refresh`.
- `bdperf probe [reset|log|on|off|nodes|hitch <ms>]` — `PerfProbe`: per-method calls/total/max (async: also wall time to completion) for about 50 deck-size-sensitive methods, and every frame over the threshold (100 ms) logged with the probes that ran in it.
- `bdperf pile [verify <n>]` — `CardModel.Pile` for every card: index vs vanilla answers and timings; `verify 1` checks every live answer against vanilla.
- `bdperf plaque [n]` — BaseLib's card type line for the first n cards of the local piles, TypePlaqueSkip off then on: same `LocString` object for every card, and the times.
- `bdperf sortkeys [wrong]` — in combat, the local draw, discard and hand cards sorted with the game's comparison (whole mod off) and with CardSortKeys, then `StableShuffle` both ways from one seed: same order, and the times; `wrong` reverses its answers (positive control). On v0.113+, then the same sort with a Veilpiercer on the local player (put on and taken off again with the game's internal calls, silently, before the bench returns; its icon shows and goes): CardSortKeys must answer, not refuse (a refusal naming an unknown keyword listener of the combat is `not compared`), with the game's order.
- `bdperf fault piles|walk` — the next filtered pile walk throws inside FastListeners' cache, or the next filtered mirror walk throws after its verdicts: tests of FastListeners' two fallbacks. `bdperf snapgameerror <n>` — the next n card reads of SnapshotCardReuse throw as the game's own code would: the piles go to the game's `From`, the part stays on (`GameExceptions`).
- `bdperf reserialize [verify <n>]`, `bdperf clonememo [verify <n>]` — LimitlessReserialize's kept/rebuilt/verified/mismatch counters (one kept list in n compared with a rebuilt one), LimitlessCloneMemo's reused/verified/options reused; a report after `verify` that compared nothing fails.
- Local co-op (`tools/autotest/coop.ps1`, see its README): two instances launched directly with `--force-steam off` (no Steam, so only the local `mods` folder loads: vanilla + this mod; each instance has its own data folder `user://default/<net id>`, the first launch needs the mods warning accepted once per folder), `--fastmp host_standard` / `--fastmp join --clientId 1001` (ENet on 127.0.0.1: 33771 for `coop.ps1`, 34000 + test slot for `parcoop.ps1` and `coop4.ps1`, so two series never share a port), `--log-file <path>` for separate logs, and `--bdtest <script>`. Script `mplobby <CHARACTER> [players] [seed]` picks, sets the run seed (default `BDTEST`) and readies up; console commands go through the action queue in co-op. The test runner fixes the `fight` command's encounter seed to 12345 (the game's is the clock in seconds up to v0.111, `Rng.CreateChaoticSeed` from v0.113: else peers desync) and answers card choices identically on both peers (deterministic selector, which also bypasses choice syncing). Imports of runs played with mods cap upgrade levels / skip unknown cards and relics. Fur Coat throws on `fight` before visiting the map (`CurrentMapPoint` null) and the combat never starts: the runner skips it when there is no map point. `-WithMods` copies the user's enabled Workshop mods into `<game>/mods_STEAMTEST` (read without Steam) and always deletes it: a normal launch reads it too.
- `AutoTest` — unattended sessions (launchers and scenarios in `tools/autotest`): on a game copy (a `steam_appid.txt` next to its exe, `-GameDir`/`STS2_GAME_DIR`) the exe is launched directly with `--force-steam off --bdtest <script>`, in a test slot's own user data with default settings, and the test mods (BaseLib, Limitless, BetterSpire2, the account's enabled mods, `BDTEST_EXTRA_MODS`; `BDTEST_MODS` replaces the list) copied into the copy's `mods_STEAMTEST` for the series; each one must log its initialization. Through Steam (`-applaunch 2868840`) only on the Steam install, which `run.ps1` refuses without a Debug build there; the tests no longer use it. A session whose runner logs nothing in 90 s is closed. Uses save profile 3 in memory only (the last-profile setting is untouched), disables tutorials there, answers card choices with the first options, quits at the end/on timeout. Script lines: `newrun <CHARACTER> [seed]`, `wait <s>`, `waitfor menu|run|combat|idle|nocombat [timeout]`, `cmd <console command>`, `play <n>` (up to n), `endturn` (waits until the game took the end of turn), `enemyhp <hp>` (in co-op: before this peer's combat is set up, else the line fails), `state [label] [optional]`, `playcard <ID>` (after `cmd card <ID> hand`), `speed normal|fast|instant` (sessions start in Fast), `marker`, `log`, `timeout <s>`, `quit`; `restsite <OptionClass>` and `eventoption <text key part>` pick a rest site / event option like a click, timed; `waitfor deckcount|restsite|event` (deck counter caught up, rest site options shown, event running); `nodes [label]` (scene node count, card previews, flying cards), `watch <s>` (previews sampled 4×/s in the background), `counters [label]` (cache/patch counters); `pilescroll draw|discard|exhaust <frames> [px]` (a combat pile view scrolled like a held mouse wheel), `language <code>` / `menuback` / `menutext <SLUG>` (menu texts per game language), `fakeconflict <Type:Method>` (simulates another mod patching a method). Sessions launched by `run.ps1`/`coop.ps1` run off-screen and muted (`--bdhidden`, `--bdmute`; `-Visible`/`-Sound` to opt out). `--bdshow <screen>` (fullscreen and on top on that screen, mute-in-background skipped), `deckfilter`; `newrun` stops the menu music as the character select screen does (it kept playing under the act music). `bdperf checks on|off` turns the Debug-only vanilla re-checks off to measure release-like timings. For dotnet-trace, keep the game alive until the trace ends (symbols are only written at the end of a trace on a live process).

- Other game versions without switching Steam's branch (2026-10-26): full copies `C:\Games\StS2-public-0.107.1` and `C:\Games\StS2-beta-0.113.1` (the two tested; `-0.111.0` and `-0.113.0` kept, no longer distributed nor tested) with a `steam_appid.txt` (2868840) next to the exe, launched directly (first with Steam initializing and the Workshop mods loading; the test runners now launch them without Steam, above): `run.ps1 -GameDir <copy>`, `coop.ps1` with `$env:STS2_GAME_DIR`, deploy with `-p:ModDeployDir=<copy>\mods\BigDeckOptimization`. Builds always target the public version (`refs/v0.107.1`, `Directory.Build.local.props`). `tools/compat` lists every member of the DLL a version lacks; `tools/fpdiff review` every fingerprinted method a version changed (diffs, new callees, renames; `hookfps` feeds `tools/hookskip/classify.py`, `describe` prints a method's fingerprinted code); `tools/mutate` writes modified game/mod assemblies for `tools/autotest/mutations.ps1`. `tools/check_versions.py` also asks Steam what is published (builds of the public and public-beta branches through SteamCMD's anonymous login, the Workshop update times of every watched mod installed (BaseLib, Limitless, BetterSpire2, SpeedX, Unlimited) through the Steam Web API), so an update players get but this machine hasn't downloaded yet shows up.
- Scenarios added for v0.6.1–v0.6.4: `guard-checks` (`faultprobe`), `started-off`, `pace-on/off/vanilla/instant` (enemy turn times vs vanilla), `menu-instant`, `coopinstant` (one peer with instant combats), `ab-2k-off/on` (all optimizations A/B at ~1,900 cards: short enough for a vanilla run on the beta), `coopbig` (co-op timings with ~3,700 cards each), `cooptry` (hands-on co-op: `coop.ps1 -HostVisible`, you play the host, the scripted fake player uses `selector local` so it never answers your card choices). Run `unlock all` once on a new test profile (the non-Steam co-op profiles `default/1` and `default/1001` are unlocked, and set to English, since 2026-10-27).

## Distribution

- Steam Workshop (primary), GitHub (MIT, private until v0.1) for source and releases, Nexus on demand.
- ID `BigDeckOptimization`, display name "Big Deck Optimization".
- README / Workshop text: don't present Limitless as the source of big decks; only list it among compatible mods.

### Lesson (stress trace 2, 2026-10-21)

Per-handler scopes cost more than they saved: each outermost scope rebuilt and re-filtered ~12k listeners for one or two hook calls (filter 7.3 s + rebuild 2.8 s per 60 s). Scopes must be coarse (one per refresh) and the filter single-pass.

### Stress results (2026-10-21, singleplayer, ~4,690 cards + ~1,840 relics = 5x the reference run)

| Build | Refreshes / 60 s | Avg per refresh | `bdperf refresh` |
|---|---|---|---|
| cache only | 670 | 27.4 ms | — |
| + HookFilter, per-handler scopes | 625 | 29.8 ms | x5.9 (hand only) |
| + single dispatch scope, single-pass filter | 798 | 16.9 ms | 152 → 14 ms, x10.7 |

Remaining per refresh (~12 ms inside the scope): run listener materialization ~2.3 ms, filter ~1.6 ms, Limitless enchantment visuals, CardCostHelper energy/star cost loops. Out of scope: gameplay hooks (damage, HP loss, after-damage...) ~20 ms per hit at this load — caching them needs lazy per-item `Contains` semantics and a dedicated verifier (desync risk), deferred to v0.2. Reusing lists across refreshes was rejected: BaseLib card modifiers can change between refreshes without a hook we can track.
